**The crust-walkers**

- Worked: tubes of rings posed on the processor each frame by a pure function of seconds, one draw for 14 animals. The first cost line said 2.12 s a sample at uhd draft (heart-3: 35.41 s with, 33.29 s without, one run each; it was 0.43 at 11:56). REPLACED by the later hand-back (13:51 UTC), which holds: MEASURED 1.0 to 2.1 s a sample at uhd draft (heart-3, two pairs: 34.33 and 35.41 s with, 33.31 and 33.29 s without; it was 0.43 at 11:56). The two runs without agree to 0.02 s; the two runs with differ by 1.08 s, on different mains, so the spread of a single run is about as large as the number itself. At or over the share of 1 s either way. Which change added it: not measured (suspects named by the owner: the `poolApron` line of C10, or the lake's loop over the 84 contact discs).
- Worked: feet on a moving floor (see the animal recipe, the step on feet). Cost not measured apart from the whole.
- Worked: few stout roots (9, forked, splayed in world directions): read as fingers with gaps. Not measured.
- Did not: 36 thin threads for roots: a fan of ribbons. Not measured.
- Worked: trunk as lumps that run no one way with pale ash on up-facing ridges. Not measured.
- Did not: long hanging folds on the trunk: draped cloth. Not measured.
- Worked: cords along the neck from noise at 0.3 cells a metre along, 60 round. Not measured.
- Did not: cords at 1.3 a metre: zigzag bands. Not measured.
- Worked: motion that reads at preview size: necks lifted 0.85 of the way to a carried place, weight shifted 0.5 k each way. No cost (STATED).
- Did not: sways of 0.2 k did not read. No cost (STATED).
- Did not: a float DataTexture for the feet: gate G10 fails. A uniform array does the same.
- Did not: a longer reach of the animal's own pool light for the belly: +3 of 255 only (the light owner's measure); the rig's `poolApron` did it.
- Trap: reach in `herd.ts` hangs on `TUFT_UP` and neckLen: change either and every tuft comes down elsewhere and a key changes.
- Trap: a GLSL name the fragment has already (ash, dust, fold, high) fails only in the render's log; and a jpg rewritten under an old name is shown stale.
- Still wrong: 1. Cost 2.12 s a sample against a share of 1 s, cause not found (the later hand-back gives 1.0 to 2.1 s; it holds). 2. Feet: the rim reads as a pale cone under a dark hoof, not as dark displaced ash, and nothing is cast on the floor. 3. Far animals: at sheet size nothing but the nearest necks is seen to move; heart-4's two are flat outlines.

**The one that sank**

Cost (MEASURED on the merge of bcbefc4 level main, not on bd5c0ac): the whole part at key heart-2, uhd draft, two runs: 2.04 s a sample above the empty frame (cap 2 s; the measure swings about 0.1 s; 6.42 s alone, empty frame 4.38 s, whole frame 25.93 s). Only the whole part was measured, 1.97 to 2.04 s; single techniques NOT measured apart.

- Worked: matte field with the crust's fresnel; tone staged by shot with `stagedSeconds()`, not by camera distance. Cost not measured apart.
- Worked: an event as a line in the vertex stage (distance to a segment, one side drops on a hinge), its face lit by `blackbody`, with two small gives before it so that a sheet at preview size changes in every cell. Cost not measured apart.
- Did not: one drop alone at frame 3740 (not seen at preview size).
- Worked: gating a bright seam by distance. Cost not measured apart.
- Did not: the same seam in the wide view (under a pixel, reads as chips).
- Worked: texture laid shorter along a rope plus ground-measure break-up against streaks at a low camera. Cost not measured apart.
- Did not: the texture at its own scale (long features foreshorten into streaks); ropy texture laid by height on steep skin (contour lines).
- Worked: grit of 1 to 3 cm switched by the NARROW side of a pixel. Cost not measured apart.
- Did not: switching grit by the long side of a pixel.
- Worked: few large flakes with ragged curled edges; a round fillet for the knee. Cost not measured apart.
- Did not: many small cells (tan strips); a sharp V knee (two sticks).
- Worked for the wide view's halo: smaller contact discs, the lake's wide sums in the sheet. Cost not measured apart.
- Did not: broad discs over young black folded skin (a dark halo).
- Did not: growing the hull by mesh scale while its lava coat stayed marked on the small shape (a smooth dark belt on the lower third).
- Did not: thicker, fewer apron ropes by a higher pile (the seam broke into chips).
- Did not save cost: swapping value noises for sines or dropping one texture tap (2.04 s before and after, MEASURED). Likely cost (STATED): pixel count of the larger hull and texture taps on the sheet, not measured apart.
- Trap: the cost tool needs two parts named or it reports 0.
- Trap, the clock (the fault found): `setTime(seconds)` gets the seconds of the PLACE, counted from the place's first frame, not of the shot and not of the film. An event timed as (frame - shot's first frame) / 60 fires in an earlier shot and is never seen where it was meant. Use `secondsInPlace(UNIT, frame)`.
- Trap, two views of one thing: what makes the close key right makes the wide key wrong. Render both keys after every change.
- Still wrong: (1) the apron is low soft swells with a woolly texture; the picture has thick distinct cords with black gaps and the hull sits in them. (2) The leg's flakes are large flat pale ribbed plates; the picture has small layered tan scales that peel, and scorch toward the melt. (3) The seam is three or four short bright lengths and a soft wisp; the picture's hot seam is one fine continuous line with vapour all along it. And the cost stands 0.04 s over the cap.

### Recipes

In every recipe: PLACE is the new place's folder under `src/places/`, KEY is one of its approved pictures, SHOT is one of its shots, PART is a part's name. The shared functions are called and never copied into a part: the rig's light (`RIG_UNIFORMS_GLSL`, `RIG_GLSL`, `rigShade`, `rigOut`, `poolApron`, `breath()` and `ctx.rig.contactDiscs` from the place's `shared/rig.ts` and `shared/glsl.ts`), the fire ramp (`blackbody` in `shared/glsl.ts`) and staging by shot (`stagedSeconds()` in `shared/stage.ts`). The place's own `shared/` folder is made once by the light's owner (recipe 5); every other owner only calls it.

**1. One more landform (from the land, the loose rocks and the crust's form)**

1. Copy as the pattern for the ground: `src/places/heart/land/height.ts`, `src/places/heart/land/index.ts`, `src/places/heart/land/shaders.ts` into `src/places/PLACE/land/`; keep the part names and `ctx.parts.built(name, ...)` once a part (the land's owner).
2. Copy as the pattern for loose rock: `src/places/heart/land/rock-shape.ts` and `src/places/heart/land/rock-shader.ts` unchanged; copy `src/places/heart/land/rocks.ts` and delete every block from "The line view" down that names a heart camera or anchor (the rocks' first owner).
3. Copy as the pattern for a baked, close-seen height sheet: `src/places/heart/lake/index.ts` (rings, bake, `RELIEF_GLSL`), `src/places/heart/lake/net.ts` (joints and named seams), `src/places/heart/lake/skin.ts` (the fragment), `src/places/heart/lake/heat.ts` (`gapeAt`, `tornAt`, `meltLevel`) (the crust form's owner).
4. Change first, ground: the layout numbers in `height.ts`: the wall's profile, `FLOWS`, `RIM`, the channel x, then one landform function (`buttressAt`, `lineCrag`, the staged butte).
5. Change first, rocks: the height function passed to `scatterRocks`, the keep-outs along the camera paths (in `put()`), and the kinds table at the top of `rocks.ts`.
6. Change first, height sheet: in `net.ts` `AUTHORED`, `OWN`, `RIDGES` and the anchors of the seams; then in `index.ts` the places given as uniforms (`uGroupSeam`, `uLineSeam`, `uMound`, `uPads`) and `FLOOR_FOCUS`.
7. Before any paint, find where each key's frame meets the land: write a vitest file that casts rays from the key's lens into `landHeight`, run it with `npx vitest run tests/<file>`, and delete it before committing.
8. Call, never copy: end every material in `gl_FragColor = vec4(rigOut(colour), 1.0)`; take contact shade through `ctx.rig.contactDiscs` for rocks over 0.3 m; give anything hot a temperature and read `blackbody`; test the shot only with `stagedSeconds()` (pattern: `during('heart-02')` in `src/places/heart/land/rocks.ts`).
9. For a staged far mass, write it in world coordinates in the key's lens frame, hide it with `.visible` from `stagedSeconds()`, and name the shot in the report. Stage foreground rocks per shot with the stage clock and stand them by hand: place, top height, half width.
10. Rocks, the second owner's way: make a dozen shapes once (rounded lump, angular block as a soft meet of 6 planes with 2 to 4 corners cut, plate), each in 6 levels that share corners; one scatter function, seeded at creation, sizes by a power law, placed in drifts with bare ground between, never one by one; each larger stone gets 1 to 5 chips on its downhill side.
11. Sink every rock 20 to 50 per cent, bank grit against its foot in the skin, darken the foot, and put a contact shade under anything over 0.3 m. Pick the level per sample from pixels across; cull under a pixel; cap the densest levels. Skin in the rock's own metres (never stretched), tone per rock, ash only on what looks up and only on paler rocks. Set tone against the measured albedo of the ground it lies on, not by eye on an older floor.
12. Size scree in pixels of the lens that sees it: a grain of 4 to 18 px with one block in forty, sunk to its middle. For a long lens, a near mass is a few overlapping stones 7 m out, tops 0.4 to 1 m under the lens, falling to the frame's bottom before the middle.
13. For a carpet of stones on a moving floor: import the floor's height GLSL and uniforms, keep the bake after the floor's own `beforeSample` (`scene.onBeforeRender`), take drift from `ctx.rig.uniforms.uTime` and who is on set from `stagedSeconds()`. Use a half float target, never `FloatType`.
14. Lobes and ropes in a height sheet (crust form owner 2): lay a lattice of lobes (cell about 3 lobes a view width), search 2 by 2; each lobe a sheet with a hashed radius, summed so later ones lie on earlier ones; edge of a sheet a step at least four cells wide with a crease at its foot; the lobe with the highest hash wears the cords; cords are arcs about a point 1.5 to 2 R behind the lobe, phase warped by smooth noise, never by the roughened outline.
15. Size ropes on screen first: at the shot's distance a rope must be 3 to 6 per cent of the frame's width, else leave it out; fade a rope out under 12 pixels along the ground. Publish phase, direction, spacing, cover, and one flag a zone for the paint, and agree with the paint which of the two draws lines there: never both. Lay falls and brims from analytic lines, not from the net.
16. Build: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` then `npm run -s build`; never build while a render or the determinism runs.
17. Look: see the shape without air with `npm run -s still -- --key KEY --tier draft --format hd --diag solo --part PLACE/land --force` (rocks: `--part PLACE/bench`, at `--format uhd`); silhouettes of the height alone with `npm run -s still -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd --diag depth --force`; beside the picture with `npm run -s compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format hd` (45 s, the land's owner) and `--format uhd` (90 s by the land's owner, 65 s by the crust form's owner) for anything gated by pixel size; then `npm run crops -- --key KEY` for native crops; `npm run sheet -- --shot SHOT --tier draft --format preview` and look at the first, middle and last frame of the shot before keeping any staged rock.
18. Cost: `npm run -s cost -- --key KEY --format uhd --tier draft --parts land bench` (the land's owner; the "alone" figure holds the empty frame, read the figure above it); `npm run cost -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd --parts bench land sky` (the rocks' owner: part names without the unit; with the unit the tool lists no parts).
19. Checks before a hand-back: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run -s build`, merge main, both again, `npm run -s determinism` (whole; 2 min by the land's owner, 85 s by the crust form's owner, it reads the built dist; must print `determinism: pass`), `npm run -s gate -- --phase 1 --only G10`, push.
20. Traps: the ground's vertex shader ignores the model matrix, so a moved or rotated mesh draws where it was built. Masks by world x set by eye were wrong by 65 m, and hd stills do not show uhd detail: probe first, check at uhd. Hand-placed stones silently fail `put()`'s camera keep-outs, and a new keep-out removes hand-placed stones too: draw the part alone after every rule change. Take every height from the height function at build time, never a number. A still at hd is 1920x1080: resize before pairing with a 1280 px reference. A quantity used by the height and by the paint, written twice, paints one lattice on another: keep one global and name it to the others. Teeth: anything narrower than 3 to 5 cells, a hard `max` or `step` on the height, or a threshold in the fragment on a per-vertex value stands as a row of teeth at uhd and is invisible at preview size; take a depth picture first.

Where the owners disagree: the land's owner judges first at hd and goes to uhd only for what is gated by pixel size; the crust form's owner and the rocks' owner judge at uhd from the start and say never to judge at preview size. The rocks' first owner copies `rocks.ts` and cuts it down; the second owner gives the ten general lines of steps 10 to 12 for building loose rock from nothing. Both ways are kept.

**2. One more fluid (from the lake's melt, the crust's skin and the fall)**

1. Copy as the pattern for molten ground: `src/places/heart/lake/heat.ts`, `src/places/heart/lake/lava.ts`, `src/places/heart/lake/outline.ts` (`src/places/heart/lake/founder.ts` only for fountains) (the lava's owner 3).
2. Copy as the pattern for a stream that falls: `src/places/heart/fall/path.ts`, `shape.ts`, `look.ts`, `index.ts`; keep the three parts (mouth, fall, surge; at COMMIT their names are `vessel-mouth`, `lava-fall`, `surge`) (the fall's owner).
3. Copy as the pattern for the cooled skin on the fluid: `src/places/heart/lake/skin.ts`; keep its header (varyings `vWorld`, `vQ` = rest position, `vNormalG`, `vAux` = fold carried, cell, metres to brim) and make the relief hand you its quantities as globals set by named functions (`foldAt`, `slabAt`, `brokenAt`); never edit the relief (the crust's skin owner).
4. Change first, molten ground: set the seams in the place's `shared/anchors.ts`, then `gapeAt` widths and the level. Change first, the fall: in `path.ts` the mouth's anchor, `LIP`, the `MAIN` points (legs down the ledges), `HALF`, `FAN_FROM`, `ARM_TAIL`. Change first, the skin: base albedo and the age patches.
5. Give the neighbour who owns the crust `bankAt` before any look work; agree that the brim is bank 0. Keep one heat function, shared by the paint, the mesh and the glow map.
6. Call, never copy: every hot thing gets a kelvin and reads `blackbody`; every material ends in `rigOut(colour)`; the stream's light is the rig's strip (an own strip lit the banks twice); shot tests read `stagedSeconds()`.
7. Split drawn from light with one flag (`drawnOnly` in `src/places/heart/lake/heat.ts`), so that motion never moves the light sum; the glow map's sum must hold within 5 per cent.
8. For a wide view, move cover and width, not kelvin, over lumps 25 to 70 m travelling 10 to 20 m/s. For a close view, send pulses along a seam toward an anchor: sin(w t + k distance + noise). Thin a far field by large lanes times a break field, with unlike brightness on a third scale.
9. Fountains: build from a table of vents of unlike share, height and offset, with one dominant; give each vent a body, a half-round of bare melt that swells with the vent's burst, at least 4 pixels wide at the widest view; give the lumps tails that lengthen with speed over size.
10. The fall: draw every mark in the stream's own coordinates (metres across, seconds since the lip, minus `uTime`) so marks ride at the flow's speed; fade fine octaves by `seenAt(metres)`; count a hot brim from the fluid's edge, not the bed's.
11. The skin, first owner's way: add bands one at a time, each with its footprint fade; measure tone in boxes against the picture (p10, p50, p90 and R over B), not by eye alone.
12. The skin, the D2 owner's way (ropy crust painted on a height field): let the form give phase, direction, spacing, depth and cover per lobe, the paint only reads them; draw a fold by abs(sin(pi * phase)), 2 to 4 cords to a fold by a hash of floor(phase); fade each band by its size on screen from the screen slope of the phase, taken once at the top of main; where the form lays nothing, make open festoons (Voronoi patches, each with a flow direction; phase = along + k * across^2, bent slightly to bunch toward the front, plus a slow noise under the base slope so no line can close).
13. The skin, continued (D2): read the photograph in (along, across) of the cord, with explicit lod from the footprint, and cut its 5 to 60 cm content on rope tops to under 5 per cent; keep every dark line in one product with a floor (0.75; 0.55 for one furrow in three seen close); tone by place, not by noise; light from the melt by the plate's and the wall's direction, never by fold normals; walls by height over the melt times broken faces; hot hairlines as level lines of noise, at least 2.5 pixels wide, dimmer when widened, and let them warm the crust beside them.
14. Build: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` then `npm run -s build`.
15. Look: `npm run compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd` and judge native crops (`npm run crops -- --key KEY`) beside the approved picture (the lava's and the skin's owners); the fall's owner uses `npm run -s compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format hd`. The part alone: `npm run -s still -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd --diag solo --part PLACE/lava --force` (the fall: `--format hd --part PLACE/lava-fall`).
16. Motion: `npm run -s sheet -- --shot SHOT --tier draft --format preview` and `npm run -s sheet -- --held KEY --seconds 6 --tier draft --format preview`; judge motion on a held sheet at 0.5 s steps, and every change at hd beside the picture (the lava's owner 4); render twelve frames in a row before handing back (the skin's D2 owner).
17. Cost: `npm run -s cost -- --key KEY --format uhd --tier draft --parts vessel-mouth lava-fall surge` (the fall's owner); `npm run -s cost -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd --parts PART` (the skin's owner). The lava's owner 3 found `npm run measure` hung for 8 minutes and timed the still with the part hidden instead.
18. Checks before a hand-back: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run -s build`, `npm run -s determinism` (must end `determinism: pass`; the skin's owner also runs `npm run -s determinism -- --part PART` for still, late, loop), `npm run -s gate -- --phase 1 --only G10`.
19. Traps: shader errors show only at render, and the frame comes out without the surface: grep the output of every render for ERROR (a name such as rim may be taken already) and re-check the relief's function signatures after every merge. After merging main the crust may have moved off the brim: render main alone before blaming your change. Any flat, smooth, level face seen under 2 degrees mirrors the sky through the rig's fresnel term whatever its albedo or roughness: shade it along its normal and fully rough. Any hard function of a per-vertex quantity draws the lattice as teeth or a comb: key paint to analytic per-pixel quantities and soften over two or three cells.
20. Traps, the fall: haze in front of the fluid (the air's smoke or your own sources) makes black crust read orange: look at the part solo before you retune the crust. Once marks ride the stream the white share moves with time and with each mark: measure it after every change, at the key's frame.

Where the owners disagree: the lava's owner 3 gives a copy-and-change recipe (steps 1, 4, 5) and owner 4 gives ten general lines (steps 7 to 9, 16); the skin's first owner gives a copy-and-change recipe (steps 3, 11) and the D2 owner ten lines for ropy crust (steps 12, 13). The lava's and the skin's owners compare at uhd, the fall's owner at hd. All are kept.

**3. One more volume of weather (from the air)**

1. Copy `src/places/heart/air/` whole (`index.ts`, `volume.ts`, `smoke.ts`, `sky.ts`, `noise.ts`, `ash.ts`) into `src/places/PLACE/air/`, and the rig's sky uniforms (`uSkyZenith`, `uSkyHorizon`, `uFill` in the place's `shared/rig.ts`, which the light's owner holds; ask, do not edit).
2. Keep the part names `sky`, `air`, `fume`, `ash`.
3. Change first the three sky uniforms (through the light's owner; in the Heart the sky is frozen), then `skyMean` in `index.ts`, then the `BODIES` list in `index.ts` (at, radius, height, lean, dense, colour, lake, floor, shots).
4. Call, never copy: the air reads the rig's lights itself; multiply each body's lake and floor light by `breath()` of `shared/rig.ts`; give a glowing fume a temperature through `blackbody`; stage bodies by shot with `shots` on a body, tested with `stagedSeconds()` (a body at 0 is skipped).
5. Keep the dome and the volume pass on the same mix(horizon, zenith, pow(up, 0.45)), so air and sky meet at the horizon in one colour, and light the smoke's tops with the same `skyMean`.
6. Keep march offsets fixed to the pixel; offsets moved on with each sample make thin vapours flicker.
7. To veil a rock at its true depth, thicken the marched air locally (`uHang` in `volume.ts`); do not use a far body of smoke for it.
8. For a tall lit smoke of streaks use streak, taper, sparse on a body (`uBodyG`).
9. Build: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` and `npm run -s build`.
10. Look: a still of each key beside its approved picture at full size: `npm run compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd`, then `npm run crops -- --key KEY`.
11. Draw one part alone to see which part makes a tone: `npm run -s still -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd --diag solo --part PLACE/fume --force`, and the same with `--part PLACE/air`.
12. Measure regions as numbers against the picture before and after each change (the same patch in both, by x and y per cent of the frame); stage by shot what hurts another key.
13. Motion: `npm run -s sheet -- --held KEY --seconds 6`; a lump must travel about its own size in 6 s to be seen. Also `npm run sheet -- --shot SHOT`.
14. Cost: render the key at uhd draft twice, with and without the change (runs differ by 0.3 s). In the Heart `npm run cost -- --parts` gave an empty list for the air, so cost was taken by rendering with and without.
15. Checks before a hand-back: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run -s build`, `npm run -s determinism` whole (must print `determinism: pass`), `npm run -s gate -- --phase 1 --only G10`.
16. Trap: the smoke pass has no scene depth; each sheet hides by one mean distance. Veil a rock with the marched air; keep a far body clear of the rock on screen.
17. Trap: a tone is often another part's. The pale sheet on the Heart's buttress was the air's bank, not the plume; found only by drawing parts alone.
18. Trap (from the fall's owner): smoke sources in front of a glowing fluid lift black crust to orange; put them behind it.

**4. One more kind of animal with its cycle and its herd (from the crust-walkers and the one that sank)**

1. Copy `src/places/heart/walkers/` (`index.ts`, `animal.ts`, `herd.ts`, `material.ts`, `skin.ts`) to `src/places/PLACE/walkers/`; `skin.ts` as it is.
2. `herd.ts` first: `CAST` (one line an animal: role, heavy, neck, wide, tall, bulk, young, tuft) and `makeBuild`. Places and headings come from the place's shared anchors (`WALKERS` in `shared/anchors.ts`); its list `AWAY` names who is not drawn in which shot, and `index.ts` tests it against `stagedSeconds()`, never the world's seconds.
3. `animal.ts`: the trunk's profile, `LEGS` and `DUTY` (the cycle), `plant()` (where a pad goes down), `pose()` (sway, steps, neck). All of it a function of seconds: no state.
4. Feet on a moving floor: `setTime` writes each foot to `uFeet`; `beforeSample` (after the floor owner's) renders the 56 x 1 target with the floor's own height function, the highest of 5 points under the pad; the vertex shader moves leg and pad below 4 m by it; a lifted foot fades to 0.1 m. Use a uniform array and `HalfFloatType`; a `FloatType` data texture fails gate G10.
5. Call, never copy: the animal's material ends in `rigOut(colour)`; it takes the rig's `poolApron` at its own share (the Heart: reach 14) and round lights at its own share; a surface that sums its light by itself gets nothing from a new rig block until its owner calls it.
6. Keep one draw for the herd: tubes of rings posed on the processor each frame by a pure function of seconds.
7. Make motion that reads at preview size: in the Heart necks lifted 0.85 of the way to a carried place and weight shifted 0.5 k each way read; sways of 0.2 k did not.
8. For one more dead animal set into a surface, with a small timed event: copy `src/places/heart/sunk/` (`index.ts`, `shape.ts`, `shaders.ts`, `noise.ts`) into the new place's folder; rename the part; set UNIT, the site, `BODY`, `LEG_A`, `LEG_B`, `FIELD` in `shape.ts` first (`UNIT` in `sunk/shape.ts`: (not found at COMMIT); at COMMIT `UNIT` is exported by `src/places/heart/shared/anchors.ts` and imported by `sunk/index.ts`).
9. Event: put it at `secondsInPlace(UNIT, FILM_FRAME)` (`secondsInPlace` is in `src/film/cut.ts`), at rest before it; give it two small gives before it so that a sheet at preview size changes in every cell.
10. Stage the dead animal's tone by shot with `stagedSeconds()`, not by camera distance; gate a bright seam by distance.
11. Build: `npx tsc --noEmit -p . && npm run -s build`.
12. Look, the walkers' owner: `npm run -s compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd` (still beside picture; without `--tier draft` it renders the review tier under another file name), then `npm run crops -- --key KEY`.
13. Look, the sunk one's owner: `npm run -s compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format hd` for EVERY key that sees it, after every change; for grain `--format uhd` and unscaled crops.
14. Motion: `npm run sheet -- --shot SHOT` and `npm run sheet -- --held KEY --seconds 6`; for an event `npm run sheet -- --shot SHOT --tier draft --format preview` and `npm run sheet -- --frames A B --tier draft --format hd`.
15. Cost, the walkers' owner: `npm run -s cost -- --key KEY --parts sky` with and without `create()`. Cost, the sunk one's owner: `npm run -s cost -- --key KEY --format uhd --tier draft --runs 2 --parts PART OTHER` (the cost tool needs two parts named or it reports 0).
16. Checks before a hand-back: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run -s build`, `npm run -s determinism` (must print `determinism: pass`), `npm run -s gate -- --phase 1 --only G10`.
17. Trap: reach in `herd.ts` hangs on `TUFT_UP` and neckLen: change either and every tuft comes down elsewhere and a key changes.
18. Trap: a GLSL name the fragment has already (ash, dust, fold, high) fails only in the render's log; and a jpg rewritten under an old name is shown stale.
19. Trap, the clock: `setTime(seconds)` gets the seconds of the PLACE, counted from the place's first frame, not of the shot and not of the film. An event timed as (frame - shot's first frame) / 60 fires in an earlier shot and is never seen where it was meant. Use `secondsInPlace(UNIT, frame)`.
20. Trap, two views of one thing: what makes the close key right makes the wide key wrong. Render both keys after every change.

Where the owners disagree: the walkers' owner compares at uhd; the sunk one's owner compares at hd for every key and goes to uhd only for grain. The walkers' owner takes cost by rendering with and without `create()` beside `--parts sky`; the sunk one's owner names two parts with `--runs 2`. Both ways are kept.

**5. One more sky with its light (from the light owner and the air's sky)**

1. Copy `src/places/heart/shared/rig.ts` and `src/places/heart/shared/glsl.ts` into `src/places/PLACE/shared/`; keep `RIG_UNIFORMS_GLSL`, `RIG_GLSL`, `rigShade`, `rigOut`. Copy `src/places/heart/shared/stage.ts` beside them for `stagedSeconds()`. This is the one copy; every other folder of the place calls these and never copies them.
2. Every surface of the place ends with `gl_FragColor = vec4(rigOut(colour), 1.0)`.
3. Copy `src/places/heart/air/sky.ts` (the dome) with the rest of `air/` as recipe 3 says; keep the part name `sky`.
4. Change first: `skyRadiance` and `skyIrradiance` (`glsl.ts`), and the three sky uniforms `uSkyZenith`, `uSkyHorizon`, `uFill` (`rig.ts`); the dome draws mix(horizon, zenith, pow(up, 0.45)) and the volume pass must use the same mix.
5. Then `skyMean` in `air/index.ts` (the Heart: 0.45 horizon + 0.55 zenith + 0.12 warm), so the smoke's tops are lit by the same sky.
6. Then the rects `GLOW`, `LAND`, `LAND_FINE`, `CONTACT` (`rig.ts`) to the new ground.
7. Then the lights the parts push into `rig.emitters` (the Heart holds `MAX_EMITTERS` 8, all taken).
8. Then strips for long sources and pools for small ones (`STREAM`, `POOLS`, `poolApron`); a round light that stands for a long source washes everything evenly.
9. Keep the fire ramp as it is: nothing hot is coloured by hand, it reads `blackbody`. Keep the names and arguments of `RIG_GLSL`; the other folders call them.
10. Stage a light that belongs to one shot by `stagedSeconds()` (the Heart's `BANK` stands in heart-02 alone and `MOUTH_GLOW` uses its place in the list outside that shot).
11. Build: `npx tsc --noEmit -p . && npm run -s build`.
12. Still: `npm run -s still -- --key KEY --tier draft --format hd --force` (check the file's time: a shader error keeps the old frame).
13. Look: `npm run compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format hd`: read the frame beside the approved picture, then measure the same patch in both (p10, p50, p90), on the same main. Then `npm run compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd` and `npm run crops -- --key KEY` for native size (the Builder's rule).
14. Motion: a held sheet for anything that moves: `npm run sheet -- --held KEY --seconds 6`, and `npm run sheet -- --shot SHOT`.
15. Cost: `npm run -s cost -- --key WIDEKEY --parts none --runs 3` before and after every block added to `emitterIrradiance` (the light's owner). The air's owner takes cost by rendering the key at uhd draft twice, with and without the change (runs differ by 0.3 s).
16. Checks before a hand-back: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run -s build`, `npm run -s determinism` (must print `determinism: pass`), `npm run -s gate -- --phase 1 --only G10`.
17. Trap: a round light that stands for a long source washes everything evenly: cut its core and give the source its own strip.
18. Trap: no branch or loop saves time here, only fewer operations; and a surface that sums its light by itself (walkers, crust skin) gets nothing from a new rig block until its owner calls it.
19. Trap (the lava's D4): a flat, smooth, level face seen under 2 degrees mirrors the sky through the rig's fresnel term whatever its albedo or roughness.
20. Trap (the air): where a view reaches the sky the volume adds that sky times what the air lets through, less the veil; change the dome and the volume together or a line shows at the horizon.

Where the owners disagree: the light's owner measures cost with the cost tool (`--parts none --runs 3`), the air's owner by two whole renders with and without. Both ways are kept.

**6. One more bed of sound**

1. STATED, not run: no Heart sound has been made, so this recipe is written from `kit/06-ASSETS.md`, `kit/03-METHOD.md` (section 7) and `tools/README.md`, and no step of it has been tried on the Heart.
2. Read "Sound" in `AGENTS.md`: sound never blocks a gate, a round or a phase, and never changes a pixel. A sound that fails after its take limit becomes an open item and that moment plays without it.
3. Give the bed an id of lower case letters, digits and hyphens (the README's example is `ribs-bed`).
4. Write the cue file `audio/cues/<id>.json` (the folder `audio/cues/` is named by `tools/README.md`; it does not exist yet at COMMIT, so make it). As `tools/lib/sound-core.mjs` reads it: `schema` `carcass/cue 1`, `id`, `description`, `seconds`, optional `promptInfluence` (0 to 1), optional `loop` (true or false), and `unit` and `kind` (`bed` or `spot`) unless the cut list already lists the cue.
5. Write the description as `kit/03-METHOD.md` says: describe the source and the space plainly (its examples: "slow surf breaking inside a huge stone arch, distant", "Thick lava folding and hissing, close").
6. Check the request without spending: `npm run sound -- --cue ID --dry-run`.
7. Make the takes: `npm run sound -- --cue ID` (optionally `--takes N`). Each cue gets at most `audio.maxTakesPerCue` takes (the key is in `config/showcase.json`). Takes wait under `out/work/sound/takes/<id>/`.
8. The tool puts each take through the level chain first, because a quiet take makes the listener invent things; then the listener is asked, twice, what it hears. Keep the take that both calls describe as the thing you asked for, with no voice and no music in it.
9. See where the cue stands: `npm run sound -- --cue ID --status`; keep one take: `npm run sound -- --cue ID --keep N`. It is kept as `audio/kept/sounds/<unit>/<id>.wav` with its record `audio/records/<id>.md`. (`npm run sound -- --cue ID --take FILE` takes one sound file in place of a made take; without `--takes N` the tool makes one take.)
10. A bed that has to run longer than one take is looped with a crossfade at a point where it measures alike on both sides.
11. Check the record against "Records of sounds" in `kit/06-ASSETS.md`: Made by, Text or description, Settings, Takes, Measured (length, loudness, peak), The listener (its answers, word for word, for the kept take), Date.
12. The command prints the row for `ASSETS.md` and does not write it: add the row to `ASSETS.md` by hand.
13. Place the bed in the cut: add an entry under `sounds` in `cut/PLAN.json` (at COMMIT `cut/PLAN.json` has no `sounds` list yet; `tools/lib/cut-core.mjs` reads `plan.sounds`), then run `npm run cut`, which writes `cut/CUTLIST.json` and `cut/CUES.md`. The README's example entry in the cut list: `{ "id": "ribs-bed", "unit": "ribs", "kind": "bed", "first": 0, "frames": 2400, "file": "audio/kept/sounds/ribs/ribs-bed.wav", "gainDb": -20, "fadeInSeconds": 1, "fadeOutSeconds": 1, "loop": true, "event": null }`.
14. Build the soundtrack: `npm run mix`. It writes `audio/mix/mix.flac` and its report `audio/mix/mix.json`. A mix made twice from the same inputs is byte for byte the same.
15. Read `audio/mix/mix.json`, do not listen: the beds sit well under the music; the music and the beds drop under every line by up to `audio.duckMaxDb`; nothing is ever louder than the voice while the voice is speaking; integrated loudness at `audio.loudnessLufs`, peaks at or under `audio.truePeakDb`; for every line `marginDb` is the level of the voice above everything else in the band where speech lives.
16. Look at the sounds as one picture: `npm run sound -- --sheet` (a waveform for each with its name and length); a status that follows new kept sounds shows that picture and says the sounds can be heard on the review page (`npm run status -- --phase N --sounds`).
17. Checks before a hand-back: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run -s build`, `npm run -s determinism` (sound changes no pixel, so it must still print `determinism: pass`), `npm run -s gate -- --phase 1 --only G10`; the gate's sound checks are advisory.
18. Trap: where the files live depends on the repo. While the repo is public no sound file, mix or music file is committed on any branch: commit only the cue file and the record as text, and return the sound files to the root as attachments. `.gitignore` keeps `audio/supplied/`, `audio/kept/` and `audio/mix/` out.
19. Trap: the listener invents things on a very quiet clip and agrees easily with a line it is shown (`AGENTS.md`): ask open questions first, on clips that have been through the level chain, and believe a fault only when a second call hears it too.
20. Trap: the sound effect tool takes no seed (the tool's settings line says so), so a lost take cannot be made again the same; keep the kept file and its record. The earlier reading of the tool's terms (`kit/06-ASSETS.md`, November 23) was that sound effects may be used inside a film and may not be made available as isolated files.

## The pick

Run once, started 2026-11-26 at 10:23 UTC, the first step of Phase 5. Five fresh agents, each on its own machine, one on each seat of the jury's fixed panel (`pick_juror_a` to `pick_juror_e`). Each received the establishing frame of each of the six places from the kept stills (`out/stills/<unit>/still-key-<unit>-0-uhd-final.jpg`, scaled to 1392 x 783, inside `critics.maxImageLongEdgePx` and `critics.maxImageMegapixels`) as `picture-1.png` to `picture-6.png`, and the text of `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` word for word, followed only by how to open the files and where to send the one line. The order is one seeded shuffle of the six places turned one step for each juror, so every juror has a different order and every place stands in five different positions. The key, which no juror saw: `out/work/pick/key.json`, copied to `scorecards/pick/key.json` once the result is written.

Orders (picture 1 to 6): a heart, spine, breath, ribs, eye, marsh. b spine, breath, ribs, eye, marsh, heart. c breath, ribs, eye, marsh, heart, spine. d ribs, eye, marsh, heart, spine, breath. e eye, marsh, heart, spine, breath, ribs.

Votes, one line a juror as it answers:
- `pick_juror_b` (seat b), answered 10:24 UTC: `picture-3.png`, which in its order is the Ribs. Its sentence: "The towering curved horn-arches and the skeletal bird creatures wading toward them make a strange, readable world at a glance, and I'd want to click to find out what it is."
- `pick_juror_a` (seat a), answered 10:24 UTC: `picture-4.png`, which in its order is the Ribs. Its sentence: "The rib-like stone arches grab me immediately, and the tiny creatures wading below make me want to explore the world beyond the frame."
- `pick_juror_e` (seat e), answered 10:24 UTC: `picture-6.png`, which in its order is the Ribs. Its sentence: "The towering curved rock arches and pale long-legged creatures on a wet, reflective plain make a striking, strange scene with real depth that makes me want to click and explore it."
- `pick_juror_d` (seat d), answered 10:24 UTC: `picture-1.png`, which in its order is the Ribs. Its sentence: "The rock arches look like enormous ribs, and the tiny creatures wading beneath them make me want to explore the shore."
- `pick_juror_c` (seat c), answered 10:25 UTC: `picture-2.png`, which in its order is the Ribs. Its sentence: "The towering curved rock spires create an unforgettable silhouette that reads instantly at thumbnail size, while the alien wading creatures and the carcass on the cliff promise deep worldbuilding worth exploring." Its first message, at 10:24 UTC, said its six files were not at the path it tried. The same agent was sent the same six files once more with one line saying so. It was not replaced and it had seen no picture before.
Result, written 2026-11-26 at 10:25 UTC: the Ribs, 5 votes of 5. Every juror answered, none was replaced, and the Ribs stood in a different position for each (4, 3, 2, 1 and 6). `deliver.cardLoop.unit` is `ribs` from here on (it was `heart`). The pick is closed and is never run a second time. The five answers, word for word, and the key are in `scorecards/pick/`.

## Decisions made by the Builder

One line each: the date, the decision and the reason.

- 2026-11-24. The start of the clock was set to 20:30 UTC, the minute the task began, with `--start --at`, so the reading done before the clock existed is counted.
- 2026-11-24. Voice: `Carcass Narrator` chosen by the Builder because the kickoff line was a placeholder. See "Voice".
- 2026-11-24. Harness scripts are plain Node ES modules under `tools/` and the film's code is TypeScript under `src/`. A small command, `npm run progress`, edits sections of this file so that logging is cheap.
- 2026-11-24. For everything new that appears in a frame the GPT Image models come first, because their terms are the clearest of the image models; another model is used where it makes the better picture. See `ASSETS.md`.
- 2026-11-24. The harness drives the browser that is already on every VM (Chrome for Testing 147.0.7727.15) through `playwright-core` 1.59.1, so the Builder's VM and every sandbox VM render with one browser and nothing is downloaded.
- 2026-11-24. Critic packs for the gemini_4_carbon family are cut to at most 1048 px on the long edge, inside the config limits: it left 11 of 60 small digits unread at 1400 x 788 and none at 1048 x 1048. Every other family gets 1400 x 788.
- 2026-11-24. Megabytes and gigabytes are decimal in every check (45 MB is 45,000,000 bytes), the stricter reading.
- 2026-11-24. Every render target is half float, in every mode and on every machine, so the half float path of G10 is the only path. Accumulating four samples in half float keeps about 11 bits, well under one step of the 8 bit output, and the output is dithered.
- 2026-11-24. The page is given only the WebGL extensions listed in `config/portable.json`, on every machine: every other extension is answered as missing before three.js sees it. So G10 holds by construction and a frame cannot come to depend on an extension only one machine has.
- 2026-11-24. The harness builds its own copy of the site under `out/work/site/` when the source hash changes, so a render never empties `dist/` with the review page in it.
- 2026-11-24. Engine rule, from the first G3 run. MEASURED: the first run of `npm run determinism` differed in 7 to 42 pixels by one level. The scene target itself changed from run to run on one page, about 60 to 120 texels by one half float step, also with the volume and the embers off, while the depth view stayed identical. Pinning the browser to one core and moving the near plane changed nothing. Fixed by changes made together in `src/spike/shaders.ts`: every texture read inside a branch or a loop names its level (`textureLod`) or takes gradients computed before any branch (`textureGrad`), and the sky's cloud reads moved out of a branch. After that every stage hash (scene, volume, accumulation, bloom, final) repeats and G3 passes. Which single read was the cause was not isolated. The rule for every later shader: no texture read with implicit derivatives inside a branch or a loop.
- 2026-11-24. The engine hash covers every file under `src/` outside `src/places/`, every file under `config/` except `webgl-caps.json`, every file under `public/` outside `public/assets/`, `package-lock.json`, `cut/CUTLIST.json`, `index.html` and `vite.config.ts`. A unit's hash covers `src/places/<unit>/` and `public/assets/<unit>/`. The spike lives in `src/spike/` and so is part of the engine hash until Phase 2 replaces it.
- 2026-11-24. The tone curve is the AgX curve as the pinned three.js ships it, applied once after accumulation in `src/core/post.ts`, with exposure, grade, bloom, grain and dither around it. The look lock in Phase 3 decides whether it stays.
- 2026-11-24. Left to Phase 2 and the look lock on purpose: multisampling inside one sample (the tiers already average jittered samples) and a depth-aware upsample of the half-size volume pass. The spike measures cost and is not tuned further.
- 2026-11-24. `info().gl.extensions` lists the extensions the page actually obtained, which by the portable filter is a subset of `config/portable.json`. `npm run caps` records the machine's whole list with a portable flag on each.
- 2026-11-24. The spike is a cost test, not a place: a ground of 512 x 512 vertices displaced by a baked 1024 x 1024 height and heat map, cracks that glow, one 2048 shadow map, a fume volume marched at half the frame's size in 32 steps, 6,000 embers on closed-form paths, bloom, grain and dither. It has one shot of 600 frames and a loop of 480 frames.
- 2026-11-24. Node here is v20.20.2 on every VM. One pinned package states Node 22.12 or later in its `engines` field and runs correctly on 20.20.2 in every command used. The default reporter of the test runner crashes on Node 20 when a test fails (`Map.groupBy is not a function`), so failures are read with `npx vitest run --reporter=json --outputFile=FILE`. Node is not upgraded, so that all VMs stay alike.
- 2026-11-24. The player (`autopilot` and `loop`) renders at the `draft` tier, in `formats.hd` when the viewport's long edge is over 1400 device pixels and in `formats.preview` otherwise. It is for looking, never for recording.
- 2026-11-24. Config validation reads the names and types of every key with a fixed name from `src/config.shape.json`, which `npm run shape` writes from `config/showcase.json` (195 keys). The open maps `settled`, `motion.byUnit`, `budget.phaseHours` and `budget.reserveHours` are checked by their own rules. Found by the tests an agent wrote: 72 nested keys could be deleted with no error before this.
- 2026-11-24. The build is published with `deploy_website`, which gives one address behind Brandon's sign-in that every later publish updates. No permanent public address is made unless Brandon asks for one.
- 2026-11-24. The start page carries one quiet "Review page" link under the play control, because the hosted address cannot be edited by hand to reach `review.html`. It is page furniture and never part of a frame.
- 2026-11-24. Every asset a page loads is a picture or a text file (JSON), because the workspace's website guide states that other binary files may fail to load by `fetch()` on the hosting. Measured on the hosted copy: JSON and a JPEG both load.
- 2026-11-24. Frames are byte for byte equal across the Builder's VM and the sandbox VMs, so shots are split across render agents freely and joined without a seam check beyond G10.
- 2026-11-24. The Phase 1 tool builders were started at 22:22 UTC, while the last Phase 0 steps ran, to use the wait. They touch no Phase 0 file, and nothing of theirs is merged before the Phase 0 gate is closed.
- 2026-11-24. Render cost. The test scene at the `final` tier does not fit the limits for the master (14.7 h against 8), the film in HD (4.4 h against 3) and the card loop (2.1 h against 1) on 16 agents. So each place is built to cost no more than the test scene, costs are measured again in Phase 2 on the blockout, and a long job runs at the highest tier that fits its limit, as `AGENTS.md` says. The picture comes first: where cost must fall, the samples of far and soft things fall before anything near the camera.
- 2026-11-24. The mix is kept as `audio/mix/mix.flac`, 16 bit, 48 kHz, stereo, with seeded dither, and its report as `audio/mix/mix.json`. A 24 bit WAV of the whole film would be about 56 MB, over `repo.maxFileMb`. FLAC loses nothing.
- 2026-11-24. Sound folders: `audio/kept/narration/<clip>.mp3` holds each whole clip as the review page plays it, `audio/kept/lines/<clip>-<line>.wav` the same take split into its lines for the cut, `audio/kept/sounds/<unit>/<id>.wav` the sounds, `audio/records/<id>.md` how each was made. The schemas of `cut/MAP.json`, `cut/CUTLIST.json` and `audio/mix/mix.json` are fixed in `tools/README.md`.
- 2026-11-24. `npm run audiomap` does its own analysis of the track, so the build does not depend on a skill being present. The audio map skill of this workspace, `user:beat-sync`, is run beside it as the cross-check, and its proposal is used for the one cut in the music.
- 2026-11-24. `npm run flows` replays the visitor's flows of `FLOWS.md` against the hosted copy after every publish. It counts drawn frames inside the page, because a VM draws a frame in seconds and a screenshot can miss the picture.
- 2026-11-24. The voice and the account's characters. The ElevenLabs account has 4 of 131,000 characters left (measured 22:52 UTC) and cannot be extended through the API. Music and sound effects made by other work on the account used about 73,700 today. The build goes on: the Reveal's clip was made with the last characters, the other seven clips and every sound effect are open items, the cut gives each unmade line a length from the measured pace, and `npm run voice` makes the rest in one run once the account has characters. Said in status 8.
- 2026-11-24. No stand-in voice. A voice from another tool is not put in the animatic or the film in place of the narrator. An unmade line is silent and the animatic shows its words on the picture, so the timing can still be read. A stand-in would be heard as the film's voice and judged as it.
- 2026-11-24. The words of a take are checked by a transcriber that runs on the Builder's machine (`faster-whisper`, model `small.en`), because the price of the account's own transcriber is unknown and the account has no characters. Two sounds that are spelled two ways are not a wrong word: `never land` heard as `Neverland`, `gods` heard as `guards`. Such a pair is listed in the take's record and passes only when every other word matches and the pair sounds alike.
- 2026-11-24. The listener's description of a voice is not used as evidence (see "The listener cannot judge a voice"). The faults step of the method is done with measures instead: clipping, the level between the lines, a gap inside a word, a line far longer or shorter than its characters allow, a word the transcriber is unsure of. The two listener calls are still made and their transcript is kept.
- 2026-11-24. The delivery tag `[slowly, gravely]` goes before each clip on `eleven_v4`. Measured on the Reveal's clip: median pitch 87 Hz and 125 words a minute while speaking, against 104 Hz and 188 for the untagged Phase 0 line and 92.5 Hz and 157 for the voice's own preview sample. The model test across models could not be run for lack of characters.
- 2026-11-24. The animatic's builder kept each plate of the pull back over the wider picture until the view has left it, instead of blending to the next picture before the view leaves it as its brief said. Accepted: the key frames of the first two reveal pictures are then drawn wholly from their own plates and nothing pops (largest change between neighbouring frames 14 percent, at the ease to a stop).
- 2026-11-24. The one cut in the music runs from bar 48 (174.24 s) to bar 58 (210.06 s), so 35.82 s of the track are left out and the film is 197.68 s (`film.targetSeconds` is 195, the limits are 170 and 215). The music is unbroken through the reveal strike and for 7 s after it, the second piece opens on a hit at its own bar line, and the film ends on the track's real ending: the fall from 218.4 s, the last strike at 224.72 s and the decay. The cut was proposed by the audio map skill and passed its own check, where the joined audio is mapped again and the beat grid has to run on across the join.
- 2026-11-24. Until a clip exists, each of its lines gets a length worked out from the measured pace of `n8`: 0.105 s a character, 0.5 s for a full stop inside a line, 0.2 s for a comma, at least 0.9 s. On the four lines of `n8` that gives 9.74 s against 9.60 s measured. Every line of `n1` to `n7` in `cut/CUTLIST.json` is such an estimate, so the cut is timed again when the clips are made.
- 2026-11-24. The script is dense: about 126 s of speech in a film of 198 s. So each place is as long as its words need and no longer (Ribs 41.8 s with the opening, Heart 28.6, Breath 23.3, Blood Marsh 23.3, Spine 21.5, Eye 20.6, Reveal with the ending 38.7), and the details of a place come in the order the words name them, not in the order of `keys`.
- 2026-11-24. The opening is one shot of 17.4 s with no cut before the first strike. The approach has no approved picture of its own, so the animatic shows the Ribs establishing picture under it.
- 2026-11-24. The voice never sounds from 0.15 s before to 0.5 s after the first strike, the middle strike, the reveal strike and the last strike, and no line is placed on the loudest passage of the first half (63.2 to 70.4 s), which belongs to the lava fall alone.
- 2026-11-24. The rise. The camera leaves the lake on a beat at 159.01 s, in the quiet, and reaches the whole titan on the reveal strike at 167.12 s in one shot: 250 km wide at 159.91 s, the skull massif at 161.3 s, head and shoulders at 163.49 s as the music comes back, the whole body on the strike. It leaves fast and slows into the strike. `Now you see it.` starts at 162.35 s, one second after the skull is whole in frame. `This was never land.` ends 0.27 s before the strike. `It was a god.` starts 0.8 s after it.

After it come the fall, about four seconds of near silence, and the last strike at 188.90 s, where the Heart's glow beats once on the body. Night crosses the body during the hold, so the glow is seen on the dark side. The picture is black from 195.73 s.
- 2026-11-24. The cut list marks eleven events on accents of the map, so the places are built to them: the bore (40.03 s), a surge of the lava fall at the top of the big peak (66.84 s), the shaft breathing out (77.54 s), the seep pool bursting on the middle strike (102.63 s), six lightning strikes on the hits of the driving stretch (116.92 to 134.85 s) and the heartbeat on the last strike. In the Spine the strikes fall inside shots, not on cuts, so a bolt is seen and not hidden by a cut.

At 0 dB the line `This was never land.`, which sits on the build before the reveal strike, came out 4.8 dB above the music in the speech band at the largest duck allowed, short of the 6 dB asked. At 1.5 dB all four lines that exist measure 6.0 dB or more.
- 2026-11-24. The voice tool, as its builder made it and the Builder accepted: the edges of each line are moved to where the sound really starts and ends, the lines of a clip are brought to -16 LUFS together and the gain is repeated until they measure that after the limiter, the limiter runs in Node with no outside tool, and every line file has its own record under `audio/records/`.
- 2026-11-24. The rise, corrected after the first animatic was looked at frame by frame. The skull massif is reached at 161.0 s and head and shoulders at 162.6 s (the entry above says 161.3 and 163.49; those are replaced). So skull, shoulders and the first rib ranges are in frame before `Now you see it.` starts at 162.35 s, and the body then opens slowly over 4.5 s and is whole on the strike at 167.12 s. `kit/03-METHOD.md` section 6 asks for a rise that is slow at the lake, fastest in the middle and settling. This one keeps that shape but front-loads it: 0.9 s of slow lift off the lake, the fastest part from 159.9 to 161.0 s, then a long settling. Reasons: the two lines before the strike need 4.5 s of a body that can be read, the fast early part is where the handoff from the place to the continent is hidden, and the slow part is where the viewer works out what the land is.

- 2026-11-25. The continent of the blockout is traced by a command, not drawn by hand: tools/trace.mjs reads the approved master, takes warm and light pixels as land, drops the cyclone and the cloud, and writes the outline as points in master pixels. 50 pieces of land, 90 seas, 7062 points. Hand tracing would not repeat and a generated outline would not match the keys. The red plume east of the hips is traced as land and the fingers and toes are not traced; both are stand-in faults that the built continent of Phase 4 replaces.
- 2026-11-25. The rise is one curve in the logarithm of the view's width, through the four windows of pullback.json at their key frames, with zero speed at the lake and on the titan frame. Its speeds at the keys come from one rule (each stretch's mean speed is the mean of the speeds at its ends, worked back from the titan frame), which gives a rise that starts still, is fastest at the first key and slows all the way to the whole body. After the titan frame the camera creeps out 3 percent to the cut to black so the hold is not a frozen frame.
- 2026-11-25. G12 is measured as: the rise is every frame from the first frame of the last place to the titan frame; it must be one shot of the cut list; the camera's height may never fall; and its climb from one frame to the next may not change by more than a quarter of itself (0.002 where it is nearly still). The kit says 'one move with no cut'; this is the number the Builder put on it.
- 2026-11-25. Every part's reference picture for G13 is taken by one rule: the part is drawn alone in the stand-in view at each key frame and event frame of its unit, the frame that flags the most pixels wins, and the solo view of that frame is the picture. The rule is in npm run parts. Picking the frames by eye would not repeat.
- 2026-11-25. The film on the page plays to a web copy of the mix made by the build (dist/mix.m4a, AAC), and the picture follows the sound's clock: when the two drift apart by more than 0.05 s the picture is set to the sound. If the browser refuses to play sound, the film plays silent on its own clock and does not stop. The FLAC stays the master for every render.

- 2026-11-25. 02:17 UTC, round 1 fix, the handoff from the Eye to the rise. One contract, `handoff.json`, lives with the same bytes in `src/places/eye/` and `src/places/reveal/` and a test fails if they differ. Either side of the cut both places draw deep water and nine rings from straight above the centre of the lake, climbing 0.6 percent a frame: the Eye ends 212 m of water above and below the middle of the frame, the rise starts with 20 km. The rise then runs up to its old peak (3.9 percent a frame at key `reveal-1`), and its largest change of rate between two frames is 2.6 percent of that peak (MEASURED). Why: two critics of round 1 read the handoff as a dead second of plain water and a visible cut, and the kit asks for a cut that weather, speed and height hide.

The clouds drift at 9 km a second so they cross about 5 percent of the frame in the hold. The numbers are in `src/places/reveal/rise.json`. The titan frame itself still shows the window of the approved picture `reveal-4` (G15 passes). Six folders (land, lake, fall, air, walkers, sunk) each export PARTS and create(ctx) and are built by six agents in parallel, each inside its own folder. - 2026-11-25. The engine rejects hidden fragments itself. MEASURED: the software renderer tests depth after the fragment shader, so every layer of triangles is shaded in full, seen or not, and a shader that discards in its first line costs a fifth. So a place that sets `depthFirst` is drawn depth-first into a depth texture of its own (each opaque shader material by a twin with the same vertex shader), and every shader material of the place gets a first line that drops a 2 x 2 block when all four of its pixels lie behind that depth. MEASURED on key heart-0 at 3840 x 2160 with the first light: 13.5 s a frame of one sample against 17.7 s, and the picture is the same byte for byte (0 of 1,555,200 bytes differ at the preview size; the first try, which dropped single pixels, changed 688 pixels through their derivatives and was thrown away). Why: it is the only way found to make cost follow what is seen, it needs nothing from the owners of the parts, and it changes no picture. A material that cuts holes joins by giving its discard alone in `userData.depthFragment`.

- 2026-11-26 04:34 UTC. The shutter. MEASURED on the Reveal's key `reveal-1` (frame 9627, final tier, 4K, this machine, the measure's own fine-detail ratio against the approved picture, floor 0.75): 0.27 with the full 180 degree shutter, 0.64 with the camera's smear held to 0.0015 of the width, 0.92 with it held to 0.0002; `reveal-2` (frame 9675) reads 1.12 and 1.52. The draft tier, one sample and no shutter, read 1.02 and 1.61. Cause, found by the Reveal's owner and confirmed here: the final tier draws 4 samples a frame, and a fast camera move spreads them as four copies of the land up to 10 pixels apart instead of a blur (picture `out/stills/status/` of status 91). A camera survey without a render counts 2,851 of the film's 11,861 frames with more than 5.76 pixels of smear at 4K, in every place, 42 pixels at the worst (frame 9627). Decided: new key `film.maxSmearShare` = 0.0002 (under one pixel at 4K). The camera's own shutter closes on a move just far enough that the move smears its subject by that share of the frame's width and no more; what moves in the world (water, cloud, animals, sparks) is still drawn across the whole 180 degrees, the engine lets the world run on by the rest. So the final tier shows what the owners compose at the draft tier, cleaner, and no judged frame loses its detail to four copies. Not taken: more samples on fast frames (a true 42 pixel blur needs about 28 samples a frame, some 30 to 40 machine-hours more for the film, and, STATED and not measured, a frame blurred that far would not hold the measure's floor at any sample count: with 4 samples it read 0.27). The floor is not touched. Code: `src/core/shutter.ts`, `src/core/engine.ts` (`accumulate`), 10 tests in `tests/shutter.test.ts`.

- 2026-11-26. - 2026-11-26 08:50 UTC. The shutter rule, completed before round 3. Found while reading version 3: the Ribs' wide key `ribs-0` (frame 1241) is passed at 16 m/s, 1.9 m over the flat, and at the final tier its near ground was drawn as four copies some pixels apart, a brushed smear; the Spine's rock at frame 7418 the same (round 15 had measured that key at 0.33). Cause: `film.maxSmearShare` bounds the camera's smear at the plane of its subject, the focus distance, and the ground under a low camera is many times nearer, so it smeared many times more. The Ribs' ground owner had met the symptom by raising the blades' course marks from 0.55 to 1.15, so that the blades carried the fine detail the smeared ground had lost; the blades were then not faint as in the approved picture. MEASURED on this machine, `ribs-0` at the final tier in 4K, fine detail against the approved picture (floor 0.75): 0.788 as merged (limit 0.0002, marks 1.15); 1.093 with the limit at 0.00001 and marks 1.15; 0.965 with the limit at 0.00001 and the marks back at 0.55. Decided: `film.maxSmearShare` 0.0002 becomes 0.00001 (a twentieth of a pixel at the subject in 4K, so ground twenty times nearer than the subject stays under one pixel) and the Ribs' `BLADE_MARKS` goes back to 0.55, faint as approved. This is the rule of 04:34 UTC doing what it was written to do (every frame as sharp as a still, nothing moving as four copies), not a new rule: what moves in the world keeps the whole 180 degrees, and the Heart keeps its full shutter (`film.fullShutterUnits`), so the approved Heart does not change. No more samples are drawn, so the render budget is as it was. The type check, the build and the test suite (914 of 914) are clean with it. Not taken: slowing the Ribs' arrival at its key (it cures one frame of one place, and the Spine shows the same smear); a rule that reads the nearest surface from the picture's depth (more engine for the same picture); judging round 3 as merged (the last round of each place would have scored a smear that one number removes). The price, STATED and not measured: a fast low pass now shows its near ground crisp frame after frame instead of streaked. All six places change at the final tier, so round 3 judges the commit with this change for every place. Brandon's switch stays as written: `film.maxSmearShare` 0 gives the full 180 degree camera blur back.

- 2026-11-26. 15:55 UTC. Guards from here on are split across render machines instead of run one after another on the Builder's machine, which a guard held for 25 to 40 minutes: the branches are merged into a candidate commit without touching the working tree (git merge-tree, each merge commit with its message and trailer), the candidate is pushed as a branch guard/<name>, and each of several render machines renders its share of the frames from the build before and from the candidate at the draft tier in the preview size, compares them byte for byte and by level, and sends the pairs back as sheets. The Builder looks at every pair beside the approved pictures and only then moves main. Tools: out/work/tmp/r27/pg (cand.sh, gorder.py, gpair.py). A frame is byte equal across machines (measured in Phase 0), and each pair is rendered on one machine in any case.

Declined and contested issues move no rung

## Notes from Brandon

His words, numbered, with the date and how each was applied.

1. 2026-11-24 20:34 UTC, in the thread: "Sorry, I forgot to share with you the attached audio. It's here in this message." Applied: the file was saved under `audio/supplied/` and its hash matches `music.sha256`.
2. 2026-11-24 20:40 UTC, in the thread: "The repo is private now. A new addendum is on main: kit/addenda/001-private-repo-and-music.md. Fetch, merge, read it and apply it now, then carry on." Applied: fetched and merged (the addendum was already in at `a218a33`), read, and applied as the session log says. The repo was measured four ways at 20:41 UTC and still read public, so by the addendum's own rule no sound file was committed then. Answered in the thread. Note 3 replaced the waiting.
3. 2026-11-24 20:44 UTC, in the thread: "Forget about the repo being public, I'll set it to private later. Just continue working make sure the audio is in there. It's totally fine." Applied: this note beats the public rule of addendum 001. The three sound folders came out of `.gitignore`, the music was committed and pushed, "Sound files" reads committed to the repo, and from here the private rules of addendum 001 hold whatever a measurement reads. The measuring before every commit has stopped. One fact was said to him once, in status 2: ElevenLabs allows its sound effects inside a film and not as separate files in a public place, so the generated sounds are the reason for the switch to private.
4. 2026-11-25 07:00 UTC, in the thread, the first of two notes: "The narration. This is the approved script, word for word. It is the same text as narration.clips in config/showcase.json and \"The words\" in kit/01-FILM.md. Use it exactly as written." Then the script:

   > n1 · the opening
   > Listen.
   > There is a land the maps leave empty.
   > I have walked it. End to end.
   > I will tell you what is there.
   >
   > n2 · the Ribs
   > The strait.
   > Stone ribs. Taller than any wall men have built.
   > At low tide, the winged things come down to feed.
   > Look up. The drowned hang in the weed. That is how high the sea returns.
   >
   > n3 · the Heart
   > The fire.
   > A lake of it, under a black skin.
   > The tall ones walk the skin, and drink from the cracks.
   > It burned before there were men. It will burn after.
   >
   > n4 · the Breath
   > The pits.
   > The land breathes here. Slow. From far below.
   > Nothing grows near them but the red weed.
   > The pale herds feed, and keep their distance. They are wiser than men.
   >
   > n5 · the Blood Marsh
   > The red flats.
   > It was blood once. It still seeps.
   > The great beasts wallow in it, and grow fat.
   > The salt keeps what it catches. Forever.
   >
   > n6 · the Spine
   > The ridge.
   > Above the clouds. Above the storm.
   > Lightning walks it every night. Spire to spire.
   > The beasts sleep inside the bone, and wait for morning.
   >
   > n7 · the Eye
   > The lake.
   > Still. Gold at the edge. Black at the center.
   > The herds drink there. They do not look down.
   > The center has no bottom. I did not find one.
   >
   > n8 · the Reveal
   > Now you see it.
   > This was never land.
   > It was a god.
   > Gods die slow. This one is not finished.

7. Review round 1, item 2, the script:

   > 2. The script is changing. This note replaces the approved script, and I am finishing it here with you. Do not make any narration in this round.
   >
   > The new direction is the witness. The narrator only states what is there, like a grim nature film from an unknown alien world.
   > - No "I", no "you", no people, no maps, no god.
   > - Nothing says outright that the land is a body before the Reveal. Lines like "the ground breathes" should sound like a figure of speech first and turn out to be literal.
   > - Eight clips, four lines each, about the same length as now, so the timing of the film does not move.
   > - Every line falls on the picture it describes.
   > - The third line of the Reveal lands on the reveal strike of the music.
   > - Short, blunt lines. Not every line is about dying.
   >
   > This is my draft:
   >
   > n1 · the opening
   > Past the last calm water, there is a shore.
   > Nothing on it has a name.
   > Nothing on it should be alive.
   > All of it is.
   >
   > n2 · the Ribs
   > The strait.
   > Blades of stone, taller than the storms that break on them.
   > At low tide the winged ones come down to feed. They do not have long.
   > High in the weed hangs one that stayed. Then the sea returns. All at once.
   >
   > n3 · the Heart
   > The fire.
   > A lake of it, under a black skin.
   > The tall ones walk the skin, and drink from the cracks.
   > Sometimes the skin gives way. The fire beneath has never cooled.
   >
   > n4 · the Breath
   > The pits.
   > The ground breathes here. Slow. From far below.
   > Red growth crowds the rim, feeding on the breath.
   > The pale herds graze the channel, and never the rim. One did.
   >
   > n5 · the Blood Marsh
   > The red flats.
   > The ground still bleeds here. No rain made this.
   > The heavy ones wallow in it, and grow fat.
   > The salt keeps whatever it catches. Forever.
   >
   > n6 · the Spine
   > The ridge.
   > Above the cloud. Above the storm.
   > Every night the lightning walks it. Spire to spire.
   > The herds shelter in the hollow beneath, and wait for morning.
   >
   > n7 · the Eye
   > The lake.
   > Still. Gold at the edge. Black at the center.
   > The herds drink at the shallows. None of them looks down.
   > The center has no floor. Something there looks back.
   >
   > n8 · the Reveal
   > From high enough, the land has a shape.
   > None of it was ever land.
   > It was a body.
   > Everything here feeds on it. And it is not finished.
   >
   > What I want from you on the script in this round:
   > - Lay the draft against the cut list: every line beside the shot it falls on, with one frame of that shot and its time in the film.
   > - Mark every line that does not match what its shot shows, or that will not fit its shot's length. For each one, give me two replacement lines in the same voice.
   > - If a shot shows something the script should mention and does not, say so.
   > - Show me this as a sheet in the thread and on the review page, and stop there. I will send changes as review notes.
   > - When I write "The script is final.", write the final words into narration.clips in config/showcase.json, log that the config now holds the new script, and make all eight clips from it, clip n8 included, at most 3 takes of a clip. Then run the cut and the mix and make the animatic with all eight clips.

   Applied: his 32 lines are in `cut/SCRIPT-DRAFT.json` word for word. `npm run script` laid them against the cut list: `cut/SCRIPT-SHEET.md`, the Script part of the review page, and the thread (status 65). 21 lines fit, 11 are marked (5 too long, 2 tight, 4 for the picture), each with two replacements checked the same way; five things shown and not named are listed. 8. Review round 1, item 3, sound effects:

   > 3. Sound effects. This is the whole list for the film: one bed, one life sound and one event for each place. Make no sound that is not on it. Beds are about 20 seconds and loop. Life sounds are about 8 seconds. Events are about 6 to 8 seconds. Every sound sits quiet under the music and the voice. Life sounds are low and breathy: no roars, and nothing that reads as an animal from Earth.
   >
   > The Ribs
   > - Bed: sea wind through huge stone arches, slow surf far off.
   > - Life: the winged colony, dry chittering and leathery wings lifting off.
   > - Event: the bore, a deep rising roar as one wall of water arrives.
   >
   > The Heart
   > - Bed: the low churn and hiss of a lava lake, its crust creaking and ticking.
   > - Life: the tall walkers, dry tapping steps on the crust and a slow hollow draw as they feed.
   > - Event: a plate foundering, a crack, a thick fold and a burst of hiss.
   >
   > The Breath
   > - Bed: one vast slow exhale from deep in a stone shaft, with dripping.
   > - Life: the pale herds, soft steps in shallow water and a few low fluting calls far apart.
   > - Event: heavy vapour pouring over the rim, a long sliding sigh.
   >
   > The Blood Marsh
   > - Bed: dead still air, a faint insect drone, slow thick bubbling.
   > - Life: the heavy ones, wet wallowing and deep slow breaths.
   > - Event: a gas dome swelling and bursting in thick brine.
   >
   > The Spine
   > - Bed: a high gale over a knife ridge, spindrift hiss, hail ticking on rock.
   > - Life: the herd sheltering in the hollow, huddled breathing and shuffling in an echoing stone tunnel.
   > - Event: a close strike, one flat dry crack and then long rolling thunder. The same sound serves every strike.
   >
   > The Eye
   > - Bed: near silence, a faint air tone and tiny laps of water at a shore.
   > - Life: the herds drinking, quiet steps and drips at the water's edge.
   > - Event: the water doming at the centre, a deep muffled swell.
   >
   > The Reveal
   > - Bed: thin high wind that fades to nothing as the camera climbs.
   > - One deep distant thud, only if the ending uses the Heart's glow.
   >
   > Make the sounds in this round, in this order, so the cap cuts the least important things first:
   > - First, one take of every bed.
   > - Second, one take of every event.
   > - Third, one take of every life sound.
   > - Last, second takes of whatever failed its check. At most 2 takes of a sound.
   > - After the first two sounds, say in a status how many credits a second of sound really costs.

   Applied: all 20 sounds of the list were made and kept, at most 2 takes each, none that is not on it, each judged by the listener tool and by measure (records in `audio/records/`). 8 are kept with a fault and listed under "Open items". Every sound plays alone on the Listen part of the review page with the take kept and its fault. The Heart's three are in the mix under the music.
