## Declined issues

- Round 1, `R4b-1` and `R4b-3`, severe: "Give the whole-body composition a short readable hold, then fade sooner" and "Trim so the whole film comes in near 3:00". Declined: the length of the ending is set by the words and the music, which are Brandon's. Clip `n8` ends at about 181 s, the heartbeat sits on the music's last strike at 188.9 s, and `kit/01-FILM.md` says the film ends on the music's own ending. 197.68 s is inside the film's limits. The cause, a locked camera, is fixed: the hold moves.
- Round 1, `R4b-1`, severe: "Start on a wide that includes the left bank". Declined: `kit/01-FILM.md`, "The shape", fixes the opening as the approach from the sea, the first part of the Ribs, with clip `n1` over it. The cause is taken: the approach is steered so the bank and the channel are in frame.
- Round 1, `R4b-3`, major, frame 8754: "keep the horizon level". Declined: the line is the edge of the deep water, on the same diagonal in the approved picture `eye-3`. The far horizon is level.
- Round 1, `R4b-3`, severe, frame 7601: "or remove it", of the carcass. Declined: the approved picture `spine-1` has the carcass across the middle and right of the frame. The cause is taken: the stand-in gets the picture's shape and the camera stops short.
- Round 1, `R4b-1`, major: "Pull farther back and center the whole body" at the titan frame. Declined at frame 10027, which shows the window of the approved picture `reveal-4` (G15). The clear space comes in the hold after it.
- Round 2, `R4b-1`, major, and `R4b-3`, severe: "shorten the post-rise orbital hold to about 10 to 15 seconds" and "the hold from 2:49 to 3:14 lingers about 25 s". Declined again, for the reason given in round 1: the length of the ending is set by the words and the music. `kit/01-FILM.md`: the camera holds on the body, and the film ends on the music's own ending.
- Round 2, `R4b-1`, severe and major: "Open place 1 directly on the wide view" and "Trim the 20-second pre-target sea approach in place 1". Declined: `kit/01-FILM.md`, "The shape", fixes the opening as the approach from the sea, from frame 0 to the end of the music's quiet swell (`preludeEnd`, 16.5 s), with clip `n1` over it. The cause is carried under "Open items": the first two seconds need something large and something moving.
- Round 2, `R4b-1`, major, frames 9939 to 10027: "Pull back slightly further or re-center slightly rightward" so the spiral clears the frame. Declined: the approved picture `reveal-4` has the cyclone in its lower right corner, cut by the right and the bottom edge, and frame 10027 shows that picture's window (G15).
- Round 19, `reveal`, `R3-2`, severe, the titan frame: "the land reads immediately as a skeleton with eye sockets, ribs, and fingers; the regularly spaced white ridges and circular basins look drawn into the map". Declined in part. That the land reads as a skeleton at the top of the rise is the film's last picture (approved `reveal-4`) and Brandon's stated choice ("The body at the top of the rise is a humanoid titan, seen directly"; note 10: "In the last frame it must still read as a body"), and R8 tests for it. Taken: ridges at one spacing and basins as clean circles, which the owner works on.
- Round 21, `spine`, the tether-kites taken for cloud, 3 severe and 2 major: `R3-1` issue 3 (severe, "the streamers are translucent flat cards with crisp glowing outlines and wobbly edges; they look like cellophane, not cloud, snow plume or spindrift", fix "make the plume a volumetric spindrift/cloud"), `R3-1` issue 13 (major, fix "replace with volumetric cloud or plume"), `R3-2` issue 1 (severe, fix "replace with volumetric spray or cloud with soft edges"), `R4-1` issue 3 (severe, fix "replace the static ribbons with deforming volumetric mist") and `R4-2` issue 6 (major, fix "replace the ribbon-like strips with turbulent wind-sheared mist"). Declined in part: the streamers are the Spine's animals, the tether-kites of `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` ("translucent ribbons that stream downwind from stalks anchored on the lee face of each spire") and of the approved picture `spine-2`, so they are not turned into cloud or mist. Taken, in the Spine's open items on the kites and on the still air: the flat cards, the hard bright outlines, the one tint, the shape that does not change in a hold, the strike that does not light them, and that nothing shows them to be alive.
- Round 24, `eye`, wind on the lake, 2 severe: `R4-1` issue 4 (fix "add continuous wind ripples across the lake surface, shifting water reflections, and slow atmospheric drift in the caldera mist") and `R4-2` issue 1 (fix "animate the pool surface with slow wind-driven ripples and gently breaking reflections"). Declined in part: the words call the lake still (clip `n7`, "The lake. / Still."), `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` sets "a clear, still evening", the approved pictures show a quiet mirror, and the critic of the match to the pictures asks in the same round to "quiet the general chop" (`R1-1` issue 4). So no wind blows ripples across the lake. Taken, in the Eye's open items on the water and on the still views: the place must not stand frozen ("held breath, not like a still picture"), so the rings from the drinkers and the risers, the doming at the centre, the swaying filaments, the drift of the mist and the reflections that move with them are made to read in a hold.
- Rounds 20, 23 and 24, the word "ear" in three fixes for animals that stand still: `breath` `R4-1` issue 6 (minor, "tail and ear motion"), `marsh` `R4-1` issue 3 (major, "an ear or head turn") and `eye` `R4-1` issue 2 (severe, "head and ear turns out of sync"). Declined in that word alone: nothing living has eyes or ears (Brandon's stated choice). The rest of each fix is taken: breathing, shifts of weight and turns of the head, out of step with each other, in the open items on each place's motion.
- Round 26, `film`, the Spine's tether-kites taken for cloud once more, 2 major: `R4-1` issue 7 (frames 7066, 7318 and 7823, "the wisps look like flat painted cards and don't seem to move at their own height; the spires read as cut-outs against the sky", fix "use volumetric cloud that drifts with parallax and wraps around the rock") and the third part of `R5` issue 2 ("the trailing clouds look like flat painted wisps", fix "replace the ribbon clouds with volumetric storm mist"). Declined in part, for the reason given in round 21: the streamers are the Spine's animals, the tether-kites of `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` and of the approved picture `spine-2`, and they are not replaced by cloud. Taken, under "Open items": that they read as flat painted cards that do not move at their own height, and that the spires read as cut-outs against the sky.
- Round 26, `film`, `R5` issue 3 (major), the titan frame 10027, its second part: "the four bright white lumbar-spine ovals and bright white red-veined pelvis sit slightly too stark and diagram-like compared to the naturalistic rock integration of the skull and ribcage", fix "soften the albedo and contrast of the white spine and red-veined pelvis under atmospheric haze". Declined in that part: the approved picture `reveal-4` shows the snow of the backbone as four white ovals and the salt flats of the hips as a white field with red veins, the brightest things on the body, and frame 10027 shows that picture (`out/stills/reveal/compare-reveal-4.jpg`, looked at again on 2026-11-26 at 13:03 UTC). Taken, under "Open items": its first part, that the Breath has no landmark that can be found from orbit.
Round 27 (round 2 of the film), `R6` issue 3 (major), declined in part. The critic's words: "this is a wide 16:9 frame with the subject in the left half and empty grey fog and sky to the right. In a vertical phone feed it would be shown small", and the fix asked: "frame tighter on the arch so it fills the frame, or compose for vertical with the spires centred and filling the height". Declined: composing for a vertical frame. The film is 16:9 in every format the config names (`formats`), and the opening is one unbroken approach to the strait as `kit/01-FILM.md` describes it and as the approved picture of the Ribs frames it. Not declined, and open with the opening's builder: that the blades are small and low in contrast in the first frame, so they must be larger in it and separate hard from the sky and the bank.

Each with its unit, its frame, the critic's words, what was tried and what to try next.

-b is pale, dusty, sandy-brown ground with sparse isolated short orange slivers and scattered pebbles, reading as desert rather than a live lava field" (3) severe, `R3-1` issue 1 (the read as filmed), frame 3477 (key `heart-1`), at the pahoehoe lava crust across the lower right and center foreground (also visible in set-A-06.png): "the ropy crust reads as smooth, clay-like procedural zebra stripes with uniform parallel ridges and no crisp basalt fractures, grit, or glassy surface variation" (4) major, `R1-1` issue 5 (the match to the picture), frame 2722 (key `heart-0`), at the lava plain, especially lower right: "the plain shows a fine repeating ripple pattern like sand instead of cooled pahoehoe slabs and plates" (5) major, `R1-1` issue 14 (the match to the picture), frame 3685 (key `heart-2`), at the ground surface: "the ground is smooth grey with combed stripes and lacks the ropy, cracked pahoehoe detail of -a" (6) major, `R1-2` issue 11 (the match to the picture), frame 4006 (key `heart-4`), at the foreground floor: "-a has a dark lava-crust foreground crossed by a bright molten crack; -b has a smooth, featureless dark band with only a faint reflection" (7) major, `R3-1` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 3685 (key `heart-2`), at the ground beside the creature's foot and the foreground crust in the right crop: "a flat, smooth grey polygonal patch with stair-stepped edges sits immediately next to the foot, while the foreground crust is heavily stretched horizontally into blurry streaks" (8) major, `R3-1` issue 7 (the read as filmed), frame 2722 (key `heart-0`), at the lava plain, active vents on the right, and the thin foreground fissure (also visible in set-A-03.png right crop): "the plain reads as a smooth grey plane with repeated ripple stamps, the foreground fissure lights almost none of the adjacent rock, and the right-side vents have soft airbrushed halos with sharp streak-like spatter" (9) major, `R3-1` issue 10 (the read as filmed), frame 3685 (key `heart-2`), at the lava plain crust in the bottom-right crop (x 0 to 17%, y 58 to 75%): "at native size the ground reads as a smooth displaced mesh with soft, stretched procedural noise and no sharp rocky micro-detail" Tried: four generations of the crust's form and skin over rounds 3 to 7. Small tilted slabs at a brim stood as tents and triangles; many small plates riding up moved away from the picture; flat plates of 3 m that lap with a rounded step read as lobes and were kept; lobes as summed sheets with cords as arcs about a point outside the lobe were kept, and cords of 16 to 22 cm seen from under 1 m up read as stripes; the skin's bands fade by pixel footprint and ropes finer than three pixels are dropped. The form's owner tested that the stripes at 10 to 25 m are the paint's lines and not the height's. Next: ropes of unlike thickness that twist, break and end along their length, in two or three sizes on one lobe, with smooth glassy slabs and real open cracks between lobes; one lattice read by the height and by the paint; a dark wet basalt at the feeding seam in place of the pale ground; a low readable ridge with one broken hot crack on the near floor of the mouth's view.
- `heart` · look lock, round 7, open at the limit `quality.lookLockMaxRounds` · the melt reads as a flat emissive gradient with razor edges that lights little · 3 severe and 3 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-1` issue 4 (the match to the picture), frame 3477 (key `heart-1`), at the molten channel running from left-centre to lower right: "the lava is thinner and dimmer, with no bright yellow core and no swirling skin, and it lights very little of the crust beside it" (2) severe, `R3-1` issue 2 (the read as filmed), frame 3477 (key `heart-1`), at the molten lava channel running through the foreground (also visible in set-A-06.png left crop): "the lava fluid is a flat, smooth orange-to-yellow gradient with razor-sharp edges, no cooling crust plates or surface viscosity, and hard-clipped highlights with no bloom" (3) severe, `R3-2` issue 1 (the read as filmed), frame 4006 (key `heart-4`), at left crop (x 42 to 58%, y 58 to 75%), the lava stream descending the dark slope (also visible mid-frame in set-B-04.png): "the lava reads as a procedural emissive shader with uniform orange-red contour outlines painted over a smooth, clay-like dark grey chute; the rock directly touching the molten stream receives almost no bounce light or specular reflection and shows no physical depth or broken crust" (4) major, `R1-1` issue 6 (the match to the picture), frame 2722 (key `heart-0`), at the lava fissures across the plain: "the glow is broken into many short, evenly bright dashes and spot fires with hard edges that light nothing around them; -a has fewer, connected crack lines that warm the crust nearby" (5) major, `R1-2` issue 5 (the match to the picture), frame 3156 (key `heart-3`), at the ground under the drinking animal's mouth: "-a has the mouth tendrils sitting in a bright lava pool with a glowing rim and smoke rising; -b has a small soft orange glow on dusty ground with only a few thin steam wisps" (6) major, `R3-2` issue 4 (the read as filmed), frame 3156 (key `heart-3`), at right crop (x 83 to 100%, y 58 to 75%), the glowing lava cracks across the ground (also visible lower right of set-B-01.png): "the lava fissures look like flat glowing orange patches with dark-red rims sitting on top of the terrain, with no recessed depth or crust and almost no light cast onto the surrounding soil and pebbles" Tried: ribbons and foundering plates, then plates as cells of a warped lattice that ride the stream whole, a core as one smooth body with its heat by distance from mid flow, the width cut in metres with the brightness given back to the glow map. Cracks as a net and as level curves read as marble and as a contour map; a thin tearing skin with cross ropes gave hard bands. A strip light from the stream's own course made the rock beside the stream 2.2 times as red as rock 20 m away (MEASURED); a wider or stronger strip on the fan, three tries, blazed the plates and left the rock unchanged and was reverted. Next: thickness at the edge (a raised dark rind with a bright under-edge and a wet contact line), a cooling skin that tears into plates on the core, a yellow core where the flow is fast, a soft roll-off into the clipped highlight, and light from the melt on the bank within 1 to 3 m by the distance to the melt's own edge; fewer, connected cracks at the wide view in place of dashes and spot fires.

- `film` · render cost, from the budget table of 2026-11-25 15:43 UTC (five frames of the Heart, MEASURED on a 4 core machine at the final engine) · at the Heart's cost the film in hd at the final tier is 107.6 h on one machine and 8.71 h across 14 machines against `sandbox` limits of 3 h, the master in 4K 283.5 h and 22.95 h against 8 h, and the card loop 2.87 h against 1 h. The Heart review clip itself was MEASURED at 34.4 to 56.3 s a frame in hd at the final tier on 4 cores, 44.1 s a frame over its 1714 frames. Tried: nothing after round 7, which closed the look lock. Next, in Phase 4 and before Phase 5's first whole-film render: `npm run cost` on every place, bring the costliest parts down (in the five cost tables of the Heart under `out/work/cost/`, MEASURED on this machine at the draft tier, `heart/crust` and `heart/land` cost most in every one, then `heart/bench`, `heart/sky`, `heart/sunk-walker` and `heart/lava-fall`), and let the tier of each long job follow the budget table as `kit/08-BUILD-ORDER.md` says; Phase 6 step 6 sends the master to Brandon's Mac if it still does not fit.
- `film` · the live page's start, MEASURED 2026-11-25 16:26 UTC on this machine (2 cores, no graphics card): from pressing "Play the film" to the first frame is 40 s with the page frozen and nothing on screen to say it is working; after that a frame of the live 960 x 540 picture takes about 0.34 s. The start was under the 30 s limit of `npm run flows` before the Heart was built. On a machine with a graphics card it is UNKNOWN. Carried to Phase 5 (the whole film, where the live page is finished): build the places one after another with a line that says so, or build a place when the film first reaches it. The Heart itself live, on the card loop's page (`index.html?mode=loop`), same machine, 16:31 UTC: 40 s before the first frame, 22.4 s for the first frame and 9.0 to 9.7 s for each of the three after it at 960 x 540, so on a machine with no graphics card the Heart does not play live, it steps. `npm run flows` reads that as its flow 6 failing (it asks for 3 frames and a screenshot); flows 1 to 5 pass on the published address. The review page's videos do not depend on it: they are rendered files. Done in part on 2026-11-25 at 23:17 UTC (`6d33ee0`): the page now builds every place the film shows one after another before the first frame, draws each once, and shows the line "Preparing the film · N of 7" on the start page while it does, so no cut waits for a place and the page is never silent. MEASURED on this machine (2 cores, no graphics card) on the local copy of the build, with `node tools/flows.mjs --start 1500000`: the line reads "Preparing the film · 1 of 7" after the click, the first 3 frames are drawn 126 s after the click at 960 x 540, and the card loop draws 4 frames in 87 s; flows 1 to 6 pass, and flow 7 on the local copy counts 2 answers of 404 and 3 film and sound loads that the simple local server broke off, which the published address did not show at 20:02 UTC. Left for Phase 5: the same flows on the published address at the next publish (the local run overwrote `out/work/host/flows.json`), and the start on a machine with a graphics card stays UNKNOWN.
- `ribs` · round 22 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the cliffs are dry and bare where the pictures have hanging weed, running water, white streaks and bright lichen · 4 severe and 1 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-1` issue 3 (the match to the picture), frame 1241 (key `ribs-0`), at left cliff: "-a has a pale, sunlit, lichen-stained cliff top over a thick curtain of hanging wet kelp, with a large weed-covered boulder base. -b has a dark, dry, striated rock wall with no hanging kelp and no lichen, plus a separate grey slab behind it" (2) severe, `R1-1` issue 11 (the match to the picture), frame 2081 (key `ribs-1`), at rock wall: "-a has wet black rock with trickling water, glossy amber kelp strands and specular droplets. -b has a dry, bark-like procedural texture with a few dull strands and no water" (3) severe, `R1-2` issue 2 (the match to the picture), frame 1546 (key `ribs-3`), at the cliff surfaces across the frame: "there are no white guano streaks running down the ledges, and the bright orange lichen patches have become dull brown smudges" (4) severe, `R1-2` issue 3 (the match to the picture), frame 1546 (key `ribs-3`), at the base of the cliff and the ledges: "the dark hanging kelp and nest seaweed are missing; the bottom band is a dark, warped, liquid-looking smear" (5) major, `R3-1` issue 6 (the read as filmed), frame 2081 (`ribs-1`), at native size, at the hanging seaweed strands and carcass legs in the left crop (also visible in set-A-04.png): "the kelp strips look like stiff, paper-thin polygonal ribbons with faceted kinks, and the carcass skin looks like soft sculpted clay with repetitive noise" Tried: The `L1` cliff was made one dark wet mass (`wetWall`) down to the weed with a pale rim, and the weed got strap fronds, a tight glint and threads of water that run by `uTime`. A kelp curtain raised to the crag's own line read as a thatch mat over the ledges, so the curtain stays 9 m lower. On the crag white now runs from every real lip (`drip`) and lichen lies in large patches (`patchL`). Left open by the owners: fronds are flat straps, the gloss is a glint and not a wet sheet, there is no stream, the weed at the crag's foot is a swirled sheet, lichen edges follow the tile and no water runs down the rock under the colony. Next: Build the weed as separate hanging blades with thickness that lie limp on the rock and pass the low sun, hung dense down `L1` from a pale lichen-stained top to a bouldered foot, and use the same blades in place of the swirled sheet at the crag's foot. Give the wet rock a broad wet sheen and one real stream with drops, not only threads. On the crag put white runs of unlike width under every roost, raise the lichen to a saturated orange-yellow with patch edges from their own mask and not the rock tile, and set small weed nests on the ledges with the sea side.
- `ribs` · round 22 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the light is flat overcast where the pictures have a low warm sun through a break in a dark storm sky · 3 severe and 4 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-1` issue 1 (the match to the picture), frame 1241 (key `ribs-0`), at whole frame, light: "-a has low warm sun breaking through from the right and behind, which picks out the arches, orange lichen and bright specular glints on the wet flats under a dark storm sky. -b is flat, shadowless overcast grey with no direct sun, no warm accents and no glints" (2) severe, `R1-1` issue 15 (the match to the picture), frame 1759 (key `ribs-2`), at whole frame, light: "-a is backlit by warm low sun that rims the creatures, makes the orange wing membranes glow and sparkles on the water. -b is flat grey overcast with no rim light or glow" (3) severe, `R1-2` issue 8 (the match to the picture), frame 2401 (key `ribs-4`), at the sky upper right and the spray: "the break of sunlight in -a is missing, so the spray has no backlight; the spray reads as flat grey CG smoke instead of bright, sunlit, glittering water" (4) major, `R1-1` issue 12 (the match to the picture), frame 2081 (key `ribs-1`), at background: "-a has arches against sunlit cloud gaps, waders on bright reflective flats, rib bones on the rock and surf at the horizon. -b has grey horn spires, a stray flying creature, dark water and no sunlit flats" (5) major, `R1-2` issue 6 (the match to the picture), frame 1546 (key `ribs-3`), at the overall light: "the light is flat and evenly bright with a pale, washed-out grade; -a has a moody storm light with dark blue-grey cloud and wet highlights on the rock" (6) major, `R1-2` issue 14 (the match to the picture), frame 2401 (key `ribs-4`), at the overall grade: "the frame is low-contrast and milky grey; -a has deep blacks in the rock, silver highlights on the water and a storm-lit contrast" (7) major, `R3-2` issue 10 (the read as filmed), frame 1546 (`ribs-3`), at native size, at the distant fog and rock silhouette in the bottom-right crop (x 0 to 17%, y 58 to 75%): "the atmospheric haze is a flat grey tint with no light variation or layered depth" Tried: The blades go from cold grey to warm ivory and the cloud has a break that turns 0.6 degrees a second, with cloud shadow from `shared/rig.ts`. The owner calls it partly done: the sunlit side is only a little warmer than the shaded one, and the break was left on the left because that is right for ribs-0. On the water a lane taken from `uSkyBreak` gave no path in the frame, so the film carries its own sparkle, glare path and `lane`. Haze steps between far masses are still small, and M3 at ribs-3 and ribs-4 (0.83 and 0.86 at draft) leaves little room for more mist. Next: Put one real low warm key through the break into the place's rig, strong enough that sunward faces are plainly warm and bright, lee faces fall dark and shadows are cast. Feed the same sun to everything that should answer it: glints and a glare path on the wet flats, rim light and light through the sails, backlit spray, and haze that the sky visibly lights. Stage the break by shot with `stagedSeconds` so that each key has it where its own picture has it, upper right at ribs-4. Then darken the sky and deepen the blacks of the rock for a storm contrast in a cool silver-grey, and measure M3 at ribs-3 and ribs-4 after each change to the air.
- `ribs` · round 22 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · rock textures stretch, smear and go soft on the cliffs at native size · 3 severe and 3 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-2` issue 1 (the match to the picture), frame 1546 (key `ribs-3`), at the whole cliff face: "the rock is a smeared, melted procedural texture with stretched and doubled blocks; the crisp layered ledges of -a with their wet, sharp edges are gone" (2) severe, `R1-2` issue 11 (the match to the picture), frame 2401 (key `ribs-4`), at the left and right cliffs: "the cliffs are smooth, dull, blobby CG masses with a visible texture seam on the left; -a's cliffs are wet and streaked with hanging golden-brown kelp that catches the light" (3) severe, `R3-2` issue 3 (the read as filmed), frame 1546 (`ribs-3`), at the foreground and mid-ground stepped cliff faces: "rock textures are heavily stretched vertically across steep faces and bevels, giving the cliff a smeared planar-projection look" (4) major, `R3-1` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 1241 (`ribs-0`), at native size, at the dark cliff face behind the carcass in the top-left crop (also visible on the left of set-A-01.png): "the rock texture is heavily stretched, wavy, and pinched horizontally, reading like melted plastic or distorted UV noise" (5) major, `R3-2` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 2401 (`ribs-4`), at native size, at the right cliff face in the bottom-left crop (x 83 to 100%, y 25 to 42%): "the cliff surface shows severe texture smearing, soft faceted geometry, and a hard-edged spray overlay cut across it" (6) major, `R3-2` issue 7 (the read as filmed), frame 1546 (`ribs-3`), at native size, at the close-up cliff blocks in the bottom-left (x 83 to 100%, y 25 to 42%) and top-right (x 42 to 58%, y 25 to 42%) crops: "the rock looks soft, faceted, and smeared with blurry procedural blotches rather than real stone micro-detail" Tried: Rock is laid by world position on three planes (blades with `mat/colony.jpg` and a second 37 m scale, crag with `mat/ledge.jpg` and `mat/rock.jpg`, stacks likewise) and all reads go by `footLevel`, since `textureGrad` cost seven times `textureLod`. A wide relief sheet on the blades gave rope and wood grain at 0.2 s a sample and was removed. The crag as thrown cells read as stacked plates, level courses read as a block wall, and what stands is courses faulted every 1.4 blocks with slanted joints and turned blocks. The owner still calls it too regular, mostly level, with no overhangs or rounded noses. Next: Find the smear first: render the crag at ribs-3 and the gate cliffs at ribs-4 with one plane at a time and with the picture level held at 0, to tell a soft blend across bevels from a too coarse level chosen by `footLevel` on steep faces. Then sharpen the blend so each face takes one plane and choose the level along the steepest axis. Put the fracture into the mesh: overhangs with deep shade, fallen blocks, a gully and rounded noses on the crag, and fracture relief on both gate stacks. Remove the visible seam on the left cliff at ribs-4.

- `ribs` · round 22 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the animals hold one pose through the holds and slide when they fly or take off · 2 severe and 3 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R4-1` issue 1 (motion), frames 1652 to 1866 (shot `ribs-04`), at the close wader and the center wader, frames 1808–1866: "Once the camera arrives, both animals hold almost exactly the same stance for about a second. (2) severe, `R4-2` issue 1 (motion), held for 6 s from frame 1241 (key `ribs-0`), at the pale animals at the foot of the left wall and along the far shallows over the six-second hold: "Several animals hold the same hunched silhouette and foot placement for seconds, so they read as posed models even while the water moves." (3) major, `R4-1` issue 2 (motion), frames 1440 to 1651 (shot `ribs-03`), at the animals on the lower and middle rock shelves, especially frames 1517–1574: "The near flyer beats its wings, but the ledge animals keep their silhouettes and posture as the camera goes by. (4) major, `R4-1` issue 3 (motion), frames 2295 to 2506 (shot `ribs-06`), at the animals taking off ahead of the breaker, frames 2391–2487: "Several bodies seem to skim off the flats without a clear weight transfer or push against the ground. (5) major, `R4-2` issue 3 (motion), consecutive frames from 2401 (key `ribs-4`), at the low flyers crossing the shallows at left and center, also visible in set-B-02.png: "The bodies translate low over the water while the extended wings and body pitch change very little, making the flyers look as if they slide along a plane." The feeders leave before the bore in ribs-06. M2 at hand-back was 23.7, 54.5, 58.1, 15.9 and 76.8 per cent at ribs-0 to ribs-4. The hand-overs name nothing on weight at take-off or on the bank and pitch of the fliers. Next: Use the joints that exist: through the held end of the feeding shot (frames 1808 to 1866) and the six-second hold at the foot of the left wall give each standing animal a weight shift, a flex of the neck and a lifted foot in its own time, with drops falling from the tuft into rings. Let a few ledge animals near the lens answer the flyby (frames 1517 to 1574) with a turn, a new foothold or a wing settling. For take-off before the breaker (frames 2391 to 2487) show planted feet, a crouch and a push with water kicked up, and stagger the departures. Give each flier its own bank, pitch and height as it clears the rocks, and check every one on a motion sheet.
- `ribs` · round 22 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the far blades are smooth paired horns with even banding, not the pictures' leaning arches, and the rock behind the colony stands too near · 2 severe and 2 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-2` issue 5 (the match to the picture), frame 1546 (key `ribs-3`), at the far rock at left: "a huge, sharp, near-looking sheer cliff wall replaces the distant curved, hazed, bird-covered arch of -a; depth and atmospheric falloff are lost" (2) severe, `R1-2` issue 9 (the match to the picture), frame 2401 (key `ribs-4`), at the far sea stacks: "the stacks are straight, conical horn spikes; -a has tall, curved, arching rock fins that lean and are veiled in spray" (3) major, `R1-1` issue 5 (the match to the picture), frame 1241 (key `ribs-0`), at arches and sea stacks: "-a has weathered, pale, sunlit limestone arches with orange lichen, nesting birds and broken rubble bases, plus surf at their feet. -b has smooth grey horn shapes with repeating striated texture, too many tusk spires, and no lichen, birds, rubble or surf" (4) major, `R3-2` issue 9 (the read as filmed), frame 2401 (`ribs-4`), at the four curved rock spires rising behind the wave: "the spires read as smooth, symmetrically paired cone-like models with soft repeated horizontal banding" Tried: The far blades were made to lean (`L4`, `L5`, `R4`, `R5`) and the gate has four tips where the picture has five. Faint marks (0.55) with fewer stones took the final-tier M3 at ribs-0 to 0.5547, so the owner raised `BLADE_MARKS` to 1.15 (0.788 after). The Builder then found the cause in the camera's shutter, which smeared the near ground (`film.maxSmearShare` 0.0002 to 0.00001): M3 at ribs-0 rose to 0.965 and the marks went back to 0.55, which is what round 22 judged. `R2` behind the crag got faint beds and was not moved. Not done: the blunt faceted tips, the arch through the middle blade, and the right gate stack is still a tapering buttress. Next: Reshape the fins in `layout.ts`: tall curved fins of unlike shape that lean into the frame, a fifth tip at ribs-4, an arch through the middle blade and rubble at the feet, with finer tips. Set `R2` further back or stage it for the colony shot so that it reads as a far, hazed, curving arch with birds on it. Replace the even banding by uneven weathering and lichen on the sunward side. Keep the blades pale and smooth at `BLADE_MARKS` 0.55 and watch M3 at ribs-0.

- `ribs` · round 22 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the one that stayed lies diagonal as a rubbery plate, not hung head down with a thin lit hide, and one crab read as having eyes · 1 severe and 3 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-1` issue 10 (the match to the picture), frame 2081 (key `ribs-1`), at carcass: "-a has a translucent pale hide sagging off the ribs, legs hanging straight down, crabs crawling on it, and warm sun glowing through it. -b has a grey-brown carcass stretched diagonally across the rock, flat-lit, with no visible crabs at delivery size" (2) major, `R1-1` issue 8 (the match to the picture), frame 1241 (key `ribs-0`), at carcass on the cliff: "-a has a sagging, hide-covered carcass hanging head down in the kelp, lit warm. -b has a small white skeleton laid flat on the rock" (3) major, `R1-1` issue 14 (the match to the picture), frame 2081 (key `ribs-1`), at native size, at carcass and rock crops: "in -b the hide is a smeared rubbery mass with blurry ghost crabs, and the rock is a noisy, smeared bark pattern. -a has crisp crabs on a thin translucent hide, and glossy kelp over wet rock" (4) major, `R3-1` issue 3 (the read as filmed), frame 2081 (`ribs-1`), at native size, at the small pale scavenger perched on the upper ridge of the carcass in the top-right crop (also visible in set-A-04.png): "the scavenger has two distinct glossy black eyes with white specular highlights, violating the no-eyes rule, and reads as a smooth sculpted CG model" Tried: The body is a relief plate from `mat/carcass-hung.jpg` turned to the ribs-05 lens, made paler (albedo 1.12, stain 0.4), with threads of water, loose ends that sway, weed strands, drips and 16 crabs that walk. It still lies diagonal on the rock. The owner says it needs a new picture or a jointed plate. After the round the Builder found what read as eyes: scavengers painted into the body's own relief picture, which cannot draw thin legs, so each became a pale lump and the gaps between its legs showed as black spots with a white rim. They are cut out of the relief (`assets-src/ribs/mat/cut-crabs.py`, commit `5c1109b7`), and the 16 crabs the film draws itself stay. Next: Make a picture of the body hung head down with legs and tendrils free, then joint the limbs so they hang and sway, which is the owner's own next step. Make the hide thin and sagging off the ribs so the low sun glows through it once the warm key exists. Draw the walking crabs sharp at native size, each a plain matt shell with wet residue, and check on the final-tier frame that nothing on the body reads as an eye. Check the body from the ribs-0 lens as well, where it now reads as a small white skeleton laid flat.

5510 (key `breath-2`), at the sinkhole wall on the right: "the massive curved pale rib-like arches and thick billowing steam are replaced by dark vertically streaked rock ledges with minimal vapour" size, at the full-size pit rim and wall crops: "the rock surfaces look stretched, blurry, and matte with smeared procedural noise, lacking the crisp, wet, pitted mineral/bone texture, sheen, and A rib that roofed the pit near the breath-02 lens read as a tent and was removed. Then judge the wall in native crops and add wet sheen, rust and moss stains and thin threads of water on the ribs.

the `blood` colour, and `farLift` for far bushes. Next: Redraw the plume as heaps of fine thread, the owner's first open line: many more and finer ribbons with no drawn midrib and edges that dissolve (try `mat/tassel.jpg`), with a wet gloss, a thick holdfast at the root and bright through-light at the edges. Give each bush its own outline and growth direction, and lower the growth on the left bank of breath-2 to dense fine brush as in the picture. In breath-4 thicken the hanging mass at the left and remove the stray frond at the top centre. Give the drop geometry `hangGrown` so drops hang from the tips, and remove the square flecks that float over the stems. Before that, once: render round 14's growth (the Breath's life code at the commit round 14 judged) beside the current one at `breath-2` and `breath-3`, at native size and against the pictures, and start from the better. - `breath` · round 20 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the water is flat brown with stamped dot glints and a cyan patch, not wind-rippled water with gold on the crests · 2 severe and 2 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-1` issue 3 (the match to the picture), frame 5242 (key `breath-1`), at the water surface across the pool: "the water is flat and muddy-brown with uniform bright pinpoint specular dots and harsh crinkled reflections instead of cohesive wind-rippled water with warm golden wave-crest highlights (confirmed at full size in set-A-04.png)" (2) severe, `R1-1` issue 9 (the match to the picture), frame 4409 (key `breath-0`), at the winding stream, foreground water, and left mineral shelves: "the water is covered in artificial speckled dot highlights with an unnatural powdery cyan-white patch in the foreground, and the wet fractured travertine shelves on the left are replaced by smooth, blobby, hard-edged ledges (confirmed at full size in set-A-02.png)" (3) major, `R3-1` issue 2 (the read as filmed), frame 4409 (`breath-0`), at native size, at the lower-left crop above the crater lip: "the flats resolve into long parallel smears with little crosswise detail, like a texture stretched away from the camera" (4) major, `R3-1` issue 8 (the read as filmed), frame 5242 (`breath-1`), at native size, at the open water in the right crop: "the highlights resolve into many similarly sized isolated gold dashes over a nearly uniform base rather than part of a varied wave field" Tried: The sea became a graded grid of 200 by 200 that mirrors the sky, the land in two steps and the waders. A continuous `rs^256` sun lobe read as a white hammered sheet and was replaced by discrete hard glints and spark cells that live a fifth of a second, in two cell sizes blended by distance. For breath-01 a green body from 8 cm of depth and a broad field of glints were staged (`uHigh`), and the ground owner made the bed blue-green. The owner still finds the glints sparser than the picture in breath-1, and `drawn` at contrast 0.5 with no glints read as sand. Next: Keep the glints discrete but place them by the slope of a wind-ripple field of two or three scales, so they gather on crests in streaks along the sun's bearing with quiet water between, and tint them warm gold on the sunlit left. Do not return to one continuous lobe. Scale the ripple with distance so the far flats stop smearing into long parallel lines. Find the pale cyan patch in the foreground of breath-0 (the staged green body or the blue-green bed through thin water) and bring it to the picture's soft teal.

the body looks dropped through the surface" (4) severe, `R3-2` issue 2 (the read as filmed), frame 6424 (`marsh-3`), at the standing adult's feet, the juvenile at right, and the animal lying across the foreground: "the legs end as blunt stumps in the same muddy texture, and the prone body disappears into the red water without displacing it; the animals look embedded rather than weighted or submerged" (5) major, `R1-2` issue 7 (the match to the picture), frame 6800 (key `marsh-4`), at native size, at the creature's facial appendages entering the water (top right) and the water surface beside its back (bottom right): "the appendages are smooth, untextured beige shapes clipping into matte mud with no ripples or reflections, and the water surface shows harsh procedural zigzag ridges instead of fluid ripples and wet specular reflections" (6) major, `R3-1` issue 6 (the read as filmed), frame 6102 (`marsh-1`), at native size, at lower-right crop, around the stalk entering the pool: "the ripples look nearly uniform up to the stalk and scarcely respond to the obstacle; the stalk appears to end abruptly in the surface" Tried: The life owner gave the hides a `wet` look (ragged dark red line, damp band, mud thrown up the flank) and a `line` look (a dark shining meniscus where a body meets brine or mud), with drops from chin and belly and a ring only at a lifted foot. A hug ring round every standing leg and the ground owner's stamped wader rings were both read by the jury as a graphic and dropped. The hero sunk to the belly lost its legs and stood in open liquid, so it stands on the ground owner's mud bank. Left open: the brine does not part round a moving leg and no mud drips from the stuck one as it heaves. Next: Give the life a surface it can write to, as both owners ask: a stamp of height and wetness in the brine and mud under each body, so the liquid rises against a flank and the mud sinks under a planted foot. With that, draw a swell and a broken wet edge round the lying body and the stuck one's flank at `marsh-3` and `marsh-4`, and a dark contact shadow under each foot. Darken and gloss the hide below the waterline, hang mud strands under the belly, and put a small meniscus where each feeler breaks the surface. Add a tight wet ring and a broken reflection at the stalk in the `marsh-1` pool, and keep rings only where something moves.
- `marsh` · round 23 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the domes read as plastic and the burst does not behave like a liquid film · 3 severe and 3 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R1-1` issue 2 (the match to the picture), frame 6102 (key `marsh-1`), at the large right-side bubble and the left-side bursting splash: "the bubble looks like an opaque, smooth plastic dome with a hard flared rim, and the splash is a flat-shaded, clay-like opaque crown instead of translucent liquid" (2) severe, `R3-1` issue 1 (the read as filmed), frame 6102 (`marsh-1`), at the large bubble on the right and the splash left of center: "the bubble reads as a polished solid dome pasted onto the pool; the splash is a thick opaque sheet with isolated beadlike droplets and little disturbance below it" (3) severe, `R4-2` issue 1 (motion), consecutive frames round 6158 (the event `burst`), at the clear dome rising from the red pool, frames 6156 to 6163: "the dome does not burst like a fluid film. Its shell goes glassy, then sinks within about 0.07 s into a flat dark smear on the surface. It throws no droplets, makes no rim splash and sends no ring wave across the pool, so the event does not land and reads as a mesh fading into a decal" (4) major, `R3-1` issue 9 (the read as filmed), frame 5940 (`marsh-2`), at native size, at right crop, small near bubble against the pool: "the dome has a soft molded highlight and almost no distinct meniscus or ring in the uniform surface texture around it" (5) major, `R4-2` issue 2 (motion), consecutive frames round 6158 (the event `burst`), at the dark patch left on the pool, frames 6161 to 6167: "the residue stays as a hard-edged dark shape with a texture-like pattern. They read as particles spawning in place rather than spray thrown from the burst" Tried: The dome was changed from a glass hemisphere to a wet film with a wide soft window broken by runs, and its foot was softened with lip froth. Tear and crown were made one event of 0.256 s, the crown a thrown sheet in fingers of two sizes with a rolled lip, and the drops fly ballistic arcs. The owner left open that the dome has no film colour and no wall thickness and that the crown is a thin sheet. Next: Take the owner's own next step first: thickness by view angle and a thin-film tint on dome and crown, and a second inner wall for the crown's sheet, so neither reads as plastic or clay. Then make the burst at frame 6158 land: tear from one point, let the film retract into a rim, throw the drops from that rim so their source and arc show, and send one ring wave out across the pool as relief. Let the dark patch spread and calm over several frames in place of a hard-edged shape. Tie each resting dome to the pool with a meniscus and interrupted ripples.

and the foreground bank: "the left mound looks like smooth melted grey clay, the right mound looks overly fuzzy, and the foreground bank is a smooth uniform lump instead of crisp granular crystalline salt crust over wet dark-red mud" (4) major, `R1-2` issue 11 (the match to the picture), frame 6424 (key `marsh-3`), at overall lighting and colour across the flats and creatures: "the palette is chalkier and flatter with pale pink-grey flats and bright whitish-grey mottling on the creature's back, lacking the rich moody overcast contrast and deep earthy wet tones of set-B-01-a.png" (5) major, `R3-1` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 5725 (`marsh-0`), at native size, at lower-left crop, pink pools and pale ground around the plant bases: "long soft streaks continue over the ground and issue 4 (the read as filmed), frame 6424 (`marsh-3`), at native size, at the ground under the feet in both crops and the bank at lower right: "there are sharp triangular facets, smeared texture, and a what stands proud, but the owner calls it still soft close to. Rebuild the near rock at `marsh-0` with fractured relief and matte mineral grit in place of the marbled swirl. Deepen the wet dark reds at `marsh-3` by number against the approved picture, and measure the cost after each step, since the land is already 7.54 s of a 16.50 s sample against an aim of 7.

joints, palm scales and a broken mouth with a thick torn wall and a hollow with pith. For the far and left-edge stalks at `marsh-3` and `marsh-4`, thin and bend the trunks and swap the pink tufted heads for sparse, dark crimson drooping fronds as in the approved pictures. Scale the main plume to the approved framing, tighten the second bud, and bring the perched insects on it up to the count of the approved picture.

Next: Make the motion large enough to read from the wide lens and judge it on the 6 s held sheet, not by the M2 number. Fur locks, albedo above 0.15 on a smooth body and a taupe hide were dropped earlier. Thin hanging feelers read as a comb, so the stuck one has a fan of fifteen thicker ones. Next: Take the owner's first open line: the hide of the three at `marsh-3` from a picture of wet folded skin, with folds in the surface itself, hair only along the back, flaky salt on the ridge and the tones lifted to the measured numbers. Remove the dark almond crease on the side of the head at `marsh-0`, because nothing living here has eyes and nothing may read as one. Find why the pale animal behind it ends on a straight vertical edge and give it a whole rounded body with a contact shadow. Vary the feelers in shape, tip and wetness so they do not share one plastic highlight.

(2) severe, `R1-1` issue 6 (the match to the picture), frame 7418 (key `spine-2`), at the foreground cliff across the left and center: "The face takes up too much of the frame and shows broad angular shards, stretched texture, and hard triangular transitions instead of tall continuous fractured slabs with rime sitting on their ledges." (3) severe, `R3-1` issue 1 (the read as filmed), frame 7418 (`spine-2`), at the whole near cliff face filling the left two thirds of the frame: "the rock is built of flat polygon facets and shards with hard straight seams; each facet has its own stretched, smeared camouflage-like texture that stops at the facet edge, so the cliff reads as a low-poly mesh, not stone" (4) severe, `R3-1` issue 2 (the read as filmed), frame 7418 (`spine-2`), at native size, at x 0-17% y 25-42% and x 0-17% y 58-75% (near cliff): "at native size whole triangles of texture are pasted at different scales and angles over each other; you can see seams, an obvious rotated patch, and texture that is smeared and soft like clay where the camera is close" (5) major, `R1-1` issue 9 (the match to the picture), frame 7234 (key `spine-0`), at the nearby rock and snow from summit to foreground: "Closely repeated scratches and thin snow streaks flatten the broad face; the target has distinct warm rock slabs, blue shadow planes, and deeper wind-packed snow on ledges." (6) major, `R1-2` issue 7 (the match to the picture), frame 7668 (key `spine-3`), at the left foreground crag: "The blurred crag is a mostly uniform dark shape, with little of the target's snow-covered ledges to identify it as near terrain." (7) major, `R3-1` issue 6 (the read as filmed), frame 7579 (`spine-1`), at native size, at x 83-100% y 58-75% (the ground on the right): "the ice and snow is a smeared horizontal streak texture with no grain, no crystals, no surface detail; it looks like a stretched texture or motion smear" (8) major, `R3-1` issue 9 (the read as filmed), frame 7234 (`spine-0`), at native size, at x 42-58% y 58-75% (snow against the spire base): "the snow is a smeared, plastic, stretched texture, and the ice curtain on the rock is made of vertical smear streaks; snow and rock have the same plastic sheen" (9) major, `R3-2` issue 9 (the read as filmed), frame 7668 (`spine-3`), at main stack face (crop x 42-58%, y 58-75%): "rock has a smooth, clay-like sheen with smeared stretched streaks, and the same material covers every stack" Tried: Spire rock is laid on three planes by world position with true-size tiles finer near the lens, and relief was put in the mesh as upright plates (64 by 21 m), beds (52 by 14 m) and blocks (23 by 8 m). Plates of one breadth alone read as a curtain, and the relief pictures at full strength read as a clay model, so they are at 0.2 far off. Snow is the crust photograph on three planes and the seam by the dead one is gone, but drawn ripples and cracks came out as stripes. Sastrugi, hoarfrost feathers and the line of prints were not reached. Next: First render the near cliff of `spine-2` at the final tier with the shutter off and with one tile level only, to tell a smear of the sampler from a stretch of the tiles and from the facets of `spireMesh`. Then smooth the normals across the plates so no straight facet seam shows, blend the tile levels by distance without turning a patch, add fine relief within 30 m of the lens, and frame the face with the height its approved picture gives it. Make the prints as dents in the ground mesh up a rising slope to the dead one, with wet black ledges and broken ice showing through the crust. Put snow on the ledges of the near crag in `spine-3`.

(3) severe, `R1-2` issue 3 (the match to the picture), frame 7668 (key `spine-3`), at behind the central pinnacle and across the right horizon: "The rear cliffs read as blocky neighbors against a nearly level dark layer, with isolated banks of mist at right. (4) severe, `R3-2` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 8090 (`spine-4`), at the two cut-out mountains and the misty shape through the cave mouth: "the far peaks are flat-lit cut-outs with no aerial perspective, a translucent ghostly shape overlaps them like a mis-layered card, and the orange sunset sits behind a night storm sky that does not light the snow" (5) major, `R1-2` issue 9 (the match to the picture), frame 7668 (key `spine-3`), at the air beside the mountain and the upper sky: "The target's diagonal spindrift is much harder to read, and the background has flat horizontal cloud bands under a much larger bright patch." (6) major, `R3-1` issue 10 (the read as filmed), frame 7234 (`spine-0`), at native size, at x 83-100% y 58-75% (the far distance on the right): "the distant spires are flat, detail-free silhouettes under a uniform purple tint; the fog is a flat overlay with no depth layers, and a dark dome with a hard edge sits in the lower right" (7) major, `R3-1` issue 14 (the read as filmed), frame 7234 (`spine-0`), at native size, at x 0-17% y 58-75% (the sea on the left): "the sea is a dark, flat, soft-smeared surface with no waves, no foam and no reflection of the lightning; a hard dark band runs along the horizon" Tried: Six far spires are drawn on the dome as leaning shaded blades, the sea was rebuilt as a billowed deck toned by number, and `spine-04` uses a plate sky with three dark fins. The owner still finds the sea at right in `spine-0` paler than the picture (about 120 against 88), the plate's sea nearly flat, and a ragged edge left by the half-size air. A soft edge on the sea cured the stepped edge in the long lens but opened holes in the wide, and a sea free to heap rolled a head over the crest for a quarter of a second, so `seaTop` is capped beside the ridge. A storm wall that follows the bend of the ridge hid the dusk band. Next: Build the far ridge as real blades in depth that step down and lean unlike one another, each softer and bluer with distance through the place's own air, instead of shapes on the dome, and in `spine-1` set them back behind the body by air and not by a wider aperture, since 0.013 there dropped the red measure to 0.30. Raise and darken the cloud sea so it buries the valley and the feet of the spires, with rolling tops and a soft lap on the rock, so that it reads as cloud and never as water. Remove the ghost shapes near the horizon and through the far mouth, the dark dome at lower right in `spine-0` and the hard band along the horizon. Keep the dusk to a thin band, restore the violet glow in the cloud behind the face in `spine-2`, and give the storm cloud sculpted masses with small violet openings.

(3) severe, `R4-2` issue 3 (motion), consecutive frames round 7665 (the event `strike-4`), at the lightning strike at the peak tip (frames 7665 to 7674): "the bolt lingers for seven frames and produces only a soft circular flare with slow sparkler-like particles while failing to flash across the ridge or foreground rock" (4) severe, `R4-2` issue 5 (motion), consecutive frames round 7231 (the event `strike-2`), at the lightning bolt and foreground snowy ridge (frames 7230 to 7238): "the same jagged bolt shape hangs on screen for nine frames with only a soft ambient haze in the upper sky and almost no directional light on the foreground ridge" (5) major, `R4-1` issue 5 (motion), consecutive frames from 7234 (key `spine-0`), at lightning bolt, right arch impact, and overall exposure (frames 7234 to 7245, also set-A-01.png frames 7015 to 7074): "the bolt lingers across five consecutive frames in nearly the same jagged path, the frame 7015 strike washes out the whole image evenly instead of directionally lighting the ridge, and sparks hover in midair 30 to 60 frames after impact" (6) major, `R4-2` issue 7 (motion), consecutive frames round 7015 (the event `strike-1`), at the lightning bolt and impact glow after the cut (frames 7015 to 7024): "the bolt remains frozen in one shape for six frames and the impact spot lingers brightly through frame 7024 without lighting the foreground peak" Tried: In the long lens the stroke was worn down to a white core in a violet sheath, the disc and rays at the foot were removed, and a bloom card (`BLOOM_PIXELS` 380 px), a hot spot and 260 embers that fly, fall and cool were added. Return strokes were made uneven (first share 1.3, later 0.45 to 0.7, gaps on whole frames). The owner reads the bloom as smaller than approved. A solid halo showed as an outline, a glow card that writes depth showed a dark disc, and a glow pool of fixed size in metres veiled the frame in the long lens. Next: Cut each stroke to one or two frames and give each return stroke a new channel shape, so that no shape stands for five to nine frames, and end the glow at the foot with the last stroke. Measure the bloom and the stroke against the approved picture of `spine-3` before changing their size, because the owner reads the bloom as smaller than approved and the critic as a halo that hides the summit. In `spine-01` shorten the far stroke, turn it violet, land it on the nearer blade and remove the round hot spot. Give the embers gravity and a life of under ten frames.

(5) major, `R1-2` issue 10 (the match to the picture), frame 8090 (key `spine-4`), at the pack along the left wall: "The sheltered animals read as a low, nearly continuous mass of similarly toned coats. (6) major, `R1-2` issue 11 (the match to the picture), frame 8090 (key `spine-4`), at the standing animal's coat and the ice rim around it: "The coat has broad pale lighting and conspicuous sheet-like locks, while the target has a selective violet edge on finer frost and darker interior tones. That same violet edge is weak or missing on the surrounding ice." (7) major, `R3-1` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 7579 (`spine-1`), at native size, at x 0-17% y 58-75% (the animal's head): "there is a small bright light-coloured spot on the dark head block that reads as an eye; the animals are meant to have no eyes. The stick bundle under it is made of identical tube-like rods with the same speckle texture" (8) major, `R3-2` issue 7 (the read as filmed), frame 8090 (`spine-4`), at the frosted plants or creatures at lower left (crop x 0-17%, y 58-75%): "they are made of the same flat feather cards as the animal, with clipped hard edges and a crinkled bump texture stamped uniformly on every blade" Tried: The herd's coat was redrawn from a photographed lock (`lock-b.jpg`, cut at 0.5, cards 0.62 as wide), with the standing one pale (tone 1.75, frost 0.2) and the lying ones cream (tone 1.05, frost 0.02), and contact discs were added by `ctx.rig.setDisc`. The dead one's wound is now a photographed scorch with no hollow and no ribs, its tendrils were rimed but are still tubes, and its fur cards and paws are as received. The soft cut alone gave dark outlines round every lock, and no snow is displaced because the floor is the ground's. Remake the dead one's paws, coat edge and tendrils from photographs, fix the claws to the toes, and remove the pale spot on its head that reads as an eye and the black spikes at the standing one's rear. Lay the dead one's pose, head and flank over its approved picture and correct only what differs. Agree with the ground's owner a dent, pushed snow and a dark contact shadow under every body, and darken the gaps between the lying ones so each reads alone, with the standing one kept at its distance.

(3) severe, `R1-2` issue 6 (the match to the picture), frame 8090 (key `spine-4`), at the ice floor from the foreground to the cave mouth: "A broad, nearly level dark sheet with diffuse reflections and a smooth snowbank replaces the chipped, irregular ice runway with pale frost, loose snow, exposed stones and bright reflected light." (4) major, `R1-1` issue 8 (the match to the picture), frame 7234 (key `spine-0`), at the cave mouth and approach in the lower left-center: "The mouth is wide and flat-topped, with a nearly uniform blue-black interior and banks hiding the threshold. (5) major, `R3-1` issue 8 (the read as filmed), frame 7234 (`spine-0`), at the cave mouth at the base of the spire (also set-A-04): "the opening is a flat, almost pure black blob with no inner wall, no bounce light and no depth" (6) major, `R3-2` issue 6 (the read as filmed), frame 8090 (`spine-4`), at cave walls and roof (crops x 0-17%, 42-58%, 83-100% at y 25-42%): "rock texture is smeared and stretched along the surface into streaky brush-like strokes, reading as a projected or stretched texture rather than ice or stone" (7) major, `R3-2` issue 8 (the read as filmed), frame 8090 (`spine-4`), at snow bank at right and the ice floor: "snow is smooth plastic-like with no grain, sparkle or footprints, and the ice floor reflects nothing of the bright cave mouth or the lightning" Tried: The canal has rock on three planes at 7 m with slabs at 19 m, an irregular mouth, icicle meshes, a broken floor mesh banked into snow, and light from the two mouths with falloff. The owner still reads fibrous walls, a floor that mirrors too little, and an outside that is not one tall spire over a snowfield. The canal picture laid round the canal gave zig-zag rings and laid along it at 4 m gave long streaks. A snow shoulder 17 m high filled the far mouth and is 5 m now, and a warm share of 0.6 in the far mouth's light turned the whole canal brown. Next: Put ribs and uneven shelves in the canal shell's mesh within 30 m of the lens and tighten the arch to the picture's irregular upright shape, with clear ice glazing the rim and thinner, varied icicle clusters in place of the regular curtain. Make the floor wetter so it mirrors the bright mouth, with chipped crust, pale frost, loose stones, grain in the snow bank and the low outcrop at right. Rebuild the landform outside as one leaning spire over a long broken snow slope with a track leading away from the mouth. In `spine-01` give the mouth a rock lip, a taller arch and an inner wall that takes bounce light from the snow.
- `spine` · round 21 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the strike lights neither the rock, the cloud, the snow nor the hollow · 3 severe and 3 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R3-2` issue 2 (the read as filmed), frame 7668 (`spine-3`), at lightning strike point at the summit of the main stack: "the white glow and spark burst light nothing: the rock face below, the ribbons and the cloud base right next to it stay the same dull tone; the bolt is a thin 2D line" (2) severe, `R4-1` issue 4 (motion), frames 7660 to 7874 (shot `spine-04`), at spire peak during the lightning strike (frames 7668 to 7718, also set-A-09.png) and the held sky afterward (frames 7738 to 7874): "the strike emits slow floating firework-like dot sparks without lighting the rock ridge or foreground silhouette for an instant, and once the sparks fade the locked shot is completely frozen with static cloud ribbons" (3) severe, `R4-2` issue 4 (motion), consecutive frames round 8091 (the event `strike-6`), at cave mouth, snow floor, icicles, and animals during the exterior lightning (frames 8091 to 8095): "lightning strikes directly outside the cave opening without casting any bounce light or rim flash into the cave interior, making the storm feel disconnected" (4) major, `R1-2` issue 12 (the match to the picture), frame 8090 (key `spine-4`), at the sky and air immediately outside the cave: "A thin, exposed strike and broad orange glow draw the eye, while the target has a soft purple light in the storm cloud, a thin orange horizon, and diagonal snow coming across the slope and entrance." (5) major, `R3-1` issue 7 (the read as filmed), frame 7234 (`spine-0`), at the lightning bolt and its bright glow on the horizon: "the bolt and the glow point light only a small patch of cloud; nearby spires, the sea and the snow get no light from it; the glow point has a hard disc and no real bloom" (6) major, `R4-1` issue 7 (motion), consecutive frames from 8090 (key `spine-4`), at cave mouth, icicles, snow floor, and animals during the exterior lightning flash (frames 8091 to 8096): "lightning flashing outside the tunnel barely illuminates the cave interior, leaving the snow path, icicles, and animal backs flatly lit and disconnected from the storm outside" Tried: Each strike carries five rig emitters, one at the tip (`TIP_RADIUS` 8 m), and `strikeGlow` adds a violet white tip bloom with a ceiling of 0.6 within about 25 m. Emitter light without a ceiling turned the struck spire to white plaster, so the light is capped and the spire was made wet and dark instead (rock albedo 0.3). The air's glow at its full ceiling behind the long lens washed the frame to pale violet and is a fifth there. In the hollow the flash enters by the two mouths with falloff, and a fill light on the herd at a flash frame was tried by an earlier owner and did not work. Next: Treat the flash as a change of exposure: for one or two frames at each of the six strikes add one directional light from the strike point that reaches the whole ridge, the snow and the undersides of the cloud, with hard shadow, then let it die over about three frames. Light the cloud from inside round the stroke over a far wider reach than the 25 m of `strikeGlow`, and keep the rock dark so it does not turn to plaster. In `spine-05` send a narrow blue-white pulse through the mouth along the floor, the icicles and the rims of the animals' backs at frame 8091, as a rim and not a fill. Keep the warm glow to the horizon band.

Make the ridge behind the first spire a connected row of smaller blades falling away, with no hooked arch and no squat tower. The huddle is drawn and breathes only in `spine-05`, and the standing one walks on after the key. The crossing runners are switched off (`WALKERS = false`) because they read as a small upright figure. Nothing in the hand-over says the motion of the animals was measured. Next: Raise the breath and the weight shifts until they read at the distance of each shot, staggered so that no two animals move together, and blow the outer locks in gusts with `uWind`. (4) major, `R1-1` issue 12 (the match to the picture), frame 7418 (key `spine-2`), at the anchor clusters and cords down the cliff edge: "The dark frost-covered clusters are crowded near the top and low on the outer edge, leaving the center-left bare; the target spaces prominent attachments down the lit face, with arcing frosted cords visibly fixed to them." Spindrift, plumes off the crests, veil, scud and flecks are in the air pass, and gust terms were added to the kites in the long lens, where nothing yet tears off whole. The owner's own open line is that between flashes the cloud flows slowly and the sea rolls but does not pour. Scud as small pale blotches read as a dirty screen, and crest plumes at 1.7 times the sky and 0.9 thick read as flat white lenses. Next: Make the motion large enough to read in a hold of five seconds: snow in streaks at three depths that cross the frame with the wind and thicken in gusts, each flake drawn as a streak along its travel so that the final tier does not copy it. Let the sea pour through the low saddles away from the capped crest, and let the cloud masses change shape and not only slide. Send the kites' gusts down their length so their outline changes within a second. Ease the camera's travel in `spine-02` so it does not run at one pace.

(2) severe, `R3-2` issue 1 (the read as filmed), frame 9221 (`eye-4`), at the dark dome rising in mid-lake: "a smooth dark shape with radial streaks, a hard outline and no wet highlight. It reflects nothing of the lit cliff, and there is no wash, foam or break where it leaves the water, so it reads as a primitive set into a plane" (3) severe, `R3-2` issue 2 (the read as filmed), frame 9221 (`eye-4`), at the small pale spikes across the water: "every spike has the same shape, lean and size, and each sits on an identical flat white disc. They look like stamped decals with no wake, no ring tied to them and no reflection" (4) severe, `R4-1` issue 1 (motion), frames 9165 to 9540 (shot `eye-05`), at the lake surface and overhead view across frames 9335 to 9540: "the swell generates overly uniform concentric ripple rings that read like a procedural texture, subsides into nearly two seconds of near-empty flat dark blue water (frames 9404 to 9506) so the event fails to land and the crane-up ends on a weak picture, and then abruptly turns into synthetic bullseye rings at frame 9540" Tried: The swell was tried as a silver disc, as a dome 8 m high with strong threads (it read as a parasol), with rings 11 m apart under the long lens and with rings 21 m apart running whole (parallel bands). It is now the water mesh itself lifted 60 m across and up to 8.5 m, with a dark near face, a pale crown, threads of run-off and broken rings 36 m apart that fade over 120 m. Each riser got a ring and a small splash that fades seen from above. The owner still finds the rings regular from above in frames 9335 to 9370, and left the clean target rings at the end of `eye-05` as the profile agreed with the reveal. Next: Give the dome the same mirror as the lake so it carries the lit cliff and a wet highlight, and break its waterline with a churned rim of displaced water that sheds outward. Make the wake uneven: rings of unlike spacing and height that overlap and break into short crests, strongest at the dome and merging into calm water, with some of that energy kept alive through frames 9404 to 9506 so the climb does not end on empty water. Vary each riser's lean, height and width, swap the flat white disc for a small mirrored ring with a short wake, and tie it to the ring that passes it. Keep the agreed end rings but ease them in over the frames before 9540 so they do not switch on.
- `eye` · round 24 (round 3 of the place), open at the limit `quality.maxRoundsPerUnit` · the animals are flat cards that stand frozen in one pose and leave no mark on the water · 3 severe and 7 major · the critics' words: (1) severe, `R4-1` issue 2 (motion), frames 8842 to 9164 (shot `eye-04`), at the four animals and surrounding shallow water across frames 8842 to 9164: "three of the four animals stand completely frozen like static models for over five seconds, the fourth locks motionless as soon as its head lowers at frame 8930, and its drinking snout produces no ripples on a completely frozen water surface" (2) severe, `R4-1` issue 3 (motion), frames 9165 to 9540 (shot `eye-05`), at the two foreground animals in frames 9165 to 9267 (also visible in set-A-10.png): "both foreground animals remain rigidly frozen like clay models as the camera cranes up past them, showing zero posture shift or reaction to the disturbance rising in the lake directly ahead" (3) severe, `R4-1` issue 5 (motion), frames 8520 to 8680 (shot `eye-02`), at the animals in the upper-left midground and the shallow water plane across frames 8520 to 8680 (also visible in set-A-08.png): "the left animal stays frozen like a statue for the entire shot, the right animal barely moves until the final two frames, and the water surface shows no wind motion or ripples around their legs" (4) major, `R1-1` issue 7 (the match to the picture), frame 8412 (key `eye-0`), at herd along the near shore: "The animals read as a dark rusty row along the shelf, mostly standing, with a few walkers out across the orange shallows. The target has pale side-lit animals scattered along the diagonal bank, with waders, drinkers, and several resting on the scree." (5) major, `R1-1` issue 10 (the match to the picture), frame 9003 (key `eye-1`), at herd bodies and reflections at their feet: "Beyond the intended setback and animal design, the lighting is flatter brown, with muddy black ends and heavy smeared reflections instead of small warm grazing highlights and wet contact details." (6) major, `R1-1` issue 14 (the match to the picture), frame 8600 (key `eye-2`), at left group and the water immediately at the drinker: "One of the two companions is upright rather than drinking, and the entry point of the main drinker is lost among regular horizontal ripple bands." (7) major, `R1-2` issue 9 (the match to the picture), frame 9221 (key `eye-4`), at two foreground animals at the bottom edge: "The left neck leans sharply inward over a narrow diagonal body, and a hard dark stripe runs along its back. The target has rounder, lower bodies, more upright necks, and softer warm light on their edges." (8) major, `R3-1` issue 3 (the read as filmed), frame 8412 (`eye-0`), at native size, at the animals standing in the shallows (x 42 to 58%, y 58 to 75%): "the animals are flat brown silhouettes with no shading or detail, no splash or ripples around the legs, and only a faint dark smudge for shadow; they look like cut-outs pasted on the water" (9) major, `R3-1` issue 9 (the read as filmed), frame 9003 (`eye-1`), at the three adult animals and the young one: "the bodies are smooth model shapes with a uniform suede skin, and there is no wetness, mud or splash on legs that stand deep in water" (10) major, `R3-2` issue 3 (the read as filmed), frame 9221 (`eye-4`), at the two foreground animal necks and heads: "a smooth, uniform sculpted look with matching blunt heads and no wet, spray or mist on the skin. They have no reflection or contact with the water below, so they read as models placed in front of a plate" Tried: The owner did not close this: cards cannot step, so heads dip and rings run at each muzzle and leg pair, with no drops. The wide herd was darkened and given contact shadows by centring the shadow map of a high eye on the shore, and the backs of `eye-05` were sharpened (lens 0.005) with skin blur and tan lowered. Large blotches or level wrinkle bands on the backs read as stripes, and sharp relief at full strength on backs failed too. Next: Give each card a second pose to blend, or build meshes, which the owner puts first: breath, weight shifts and head dips out of step between animals, and in `eye-05` a slow lift of the neck in the two near animals as the swell rises. Then mark the water: legs dark and wet to the knee, a broken reflection under each body, and one clear ring at each muzzle that is not lost in the general bands. Light them from the low warm sun with a warm left edge and cool fill, put dust and unevenness on the hide, and soften the hard dark stripe along the near backs. Keep every distance as staged, break the row at eye-0 into scattered waders, drinkers and resting animals along the slope, and put both companions at eye-2 in a low drinking pose.

Next: Put the stream back at bottom left of eye-0, running white between the left boulders into the lake, on a continuous slope of graded scree in place of the flat grey shelf. Replace the few large blocks with many smaller ones of irregular fracture, sizes spread from boulder down to gravel, textured at true scale so that no face stretches, with growth in the crevices. At eye-1 lower or shift the camera to the waterline so the cobbles are a lip at lower left and the centre and right are open flooded shallows, with the animals kept at their staged distance. Vary pebble shape, size and stone so the soft foreground does not read as copied clay lumps.

(5) major, `R3-1` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 8412 (`eye-0`), at native size, at the top of the crop where the cliff reflection meets the orange water: "the cliff reflection ends in vertical smeared streaks and a hard horizontal edge" (6) major, `R3-1` issue 6 (the read as filmed), frame 8600 (`eye-2`), at native size, at the far water band (x 83 to 100%, y 25 to 42%) and set-A-05.png at the same region: "the water is made of horizontal stretched streaks with smeared texture, like a texture stretched along one axis, with no readable ripple structure" (7) major, `R3-2` issue 5 (the read as filmed), frame 9221 (`eye-4`), at native size, at the near water reflections (x 42 to 58%, y 58 to 75%): "the sun glints break into blocky, stair-stepped rectangles and horizontal streak banding. Filter the surface slope to the pixel so that glints stay round and do not break into steps and streaks, and blend the far edge of the cliff reflection. Cool the base water to slate at eye-4 and let gold come only from the reflected cliff.

Remove the flat pale blobs or give them a soft wet margin.

(4) major, `R1-1` issue 13 (the match to the picture), frame 8600 (key `eye-2`), at far lake edge and crater wall: "The air has become a thin pale line under an unusually crisp, busy wall, rather than a broad soft bank of vapor that obscures the base and separates the warm upper ledges from the cooler lake." (6) major, `R3-2` issue 4 (the read as filmed), frame 9221 (`eye-4`), at native size, at the fog band where it meets the water (x 0 to 100%, y 25 to 42%): "the fog is one flat grey tint laid in a horizontal strip, with a ruler-straight lower edge and a hard thin orange line at the waterline. From 450 km it stands in streets: `mat/streaks.jpg` gates the dots of `mat/specks.jpg` and is itself the thin cloud, a second lay crosses on another wind, and only the body of the first lay throws shadows from orbit. Round 19's critics called that look confetti and sprayed paint, so the item stays open. Next: Draw the sea cloud as clusters with a body: rounded soft masses with a lit and a shaded side under the place's sun, in rows trending north-east as the pictures for `reveal-2` and `reveal-3` have them, keeping the dots only as the fine top so M3 holds. Look at the final-tier frames of `e7991a3e` for what is left of the straight cutout seams, the square shadow blocks at the left of `reveal-4` and the hard-edged grey-tan blotches where cloud is drawn dark against the glint, and remove it. If the tiles cannot carry it, make a picture of a cumulus field seen from above.

eroded mountain ridges (also in set-C-02.png bottom-left, set-C-03.png right, and set-C-05.png bottom-left): "at native 4K size the terrain is soft and slightly mushy, reading as a single uniform reveal, especially the repeated ridges flanking the central valley: "The nearly uniform bright ridges on either side of the valley retain the same sharp contrast all the way out to orbit, so the Next: Put relief of two more kinds into the maps themselves, dendritic valleys that gather into trunks and fans at their mouths, so the country beside the bones stops reading as parallel stripes from one valley.
